Abstract (英文): Remote sensing image captioning enables automated interpretation of remote sensing data for applications such as environmental monitoring and urban planning. However, general-purpose multimodal models exhibit suboptimal adaptability on remote sensing imagery due to domain discrepancies in data distribution, resolution, and rotation invariance. We propose a domain-specific memory bank-enhanced framework for remote sensing image caption, built upon the lightweight EVCap architecture with three core innovations: (1) a remote sensing specific visual memory bank providing domain-representative visual-semantic alignment; (2) a staged transfer training strategy that leverages pre-trained knowledge while mitigating overfitting from limited remote sensing data; and (3) a K-means clustering-based few-shot memory bank that preserves performance while reducing memory footprint for resource-constrained deployment. Extensive experiments on NWPU, RSICD, and Sydney datasets demonstrate substantial improvements over baselines. Specifically, CIDEr increases by 3.3% on RSICD (0.2735 to 0.2825) and 11.7% on Sydney (0.1422 to 0.1589). The proposed method also achieves competitive performance against state-of-the-art methods while maintaining favorable computational efficiency.

Abstract (英文): Coastal water levels during storms result from interacting tides, storm surges, waves, currents, bathymetry, and coastal geometry, producing spatially heterogeneous responses that remain difficult to characterize from point observations alone. The Surface Water and Ocean Topography (SWOT) mission provides a new opportunity to investigate this complexity through wide-swath High-Resolution (HR) observations of sea surface height (SSH). Here, SWOT HR water levels are first evaluated against five tide gauges in the English Channel and subsequently analyzed at three contrasting sites, Cherbourg, Villers-sur-Mer, and Étretat, using complementary spatial and scale-resolved approaches. SWOT HR shows strong agreement with tide gauges at the best-performing sites, with RMSE values of approximately 0.11 m and correlations exceeding 0.99, while accuracy remains site-dependent. Beyond point-based validation, the two-dimensional HR fields reveal pronounced spatial heterogeneity, including localized and elongated SSH anomalies with distinct geometries and orientations. EOF decomposition shows that more than half of the variance remains beyond the first six modes at all three sites, highlighting the complexity of the HR spatial signal. The detrended SSH anomalies show organized spatial patterns, while their point-by-point linear correlations with radar backscatter (σ0) remain close to zero. Across the three investigated coastal domains and two storm events, the two-dimensional wavelet analysis identifies enhanced SSH variability within an energetic scale range of approximately 0.5–1.0 km, although its intensity, spatial distribution, and organization differ substantially among sites. Comparison with independent wave, surface-current, and bathymetric fields further places these observations within contrasting hydrodynamic and morphological environments, suggesting that the observed sub-kilometer SSH variability may be associated with local coastal processes, although their individual contributions remain unresolved. . Together, these analyses demonstrate the added value of SWOT HR for resolving the two-dimensional, multiscale complexity of storm-time coastal SSH, providing new spatial information for investigating coastal dynamics and supporting high-resolution model validation and data assimilation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate monitoring of river water surface elevation (WSE) is important for hydrologic and flood applications, particularly where in situ observations are limited. This study evaluates Sentinel-1 Synthetic Aperture Radar (SAR) for estimating river WSE using a river inundation area–water surface elevation (RIA–WSE) relationship derived from high-resolution topobathymetric elevation data and investigates environmental factors affecting estimation accuracy. A total of 265 Sentinel-1 SAR images acquired during 2015–2026 covering the USGS Thebes gauging station along the Mississippi River were analyzed using six VV- and VH-based backscatter metrics, yielding 1590 WSE estimates. Comparison with a conventional 1-m digital elevation model (DEM) revealed that its representation of river water-surface elevations at the time of the elevation survey, rather than submerged channel-bed elevations, limits its applicability for estimating WSE at low river stages. Among the six metrics, log10(VV) produced the best overall performance, with an RMSE of 1.61 m, mean error of −0.96 m, R2 of 0.81, and percentage of lowest absolute error (PLE) of 44.5%. A sensitivity analysis using SAR image grid spacings of 10, 5, 2, and 1 m showed that log10(VV) maintained relatively stable estimation accuracy, whereas finer resampling did not consistently improve WSE estimates. Errors were smallest under low-flow conditions (WSE < 97.0 m), largest under medium- to high-flow conditions (97.0–102.5 m) and decreased again during flooding (WSE > 102.5 m). Analysis of 10 National Agricultural Imagery Program (NAIP) images revealed increasing vegetation along the inundation boundary above approximately 100 m WSE. Under medium- to high-flow conditions, calm conditions generally produced smaller errors than non-calm conditions, although errors did not increase monotonically with wind speed. E–W and SE–NW wind orientations generally produced larger errors than N–S and NE–SW orientations. These results indicate that WSE estimation accuracy reflects combined effects of vegetation, wind-generated water surface roughness, wind orientation, effective fetch, water depth, and river morphology. Although log10(VV) performed best overall, it provided the lowest absolute error for only 44.5% of acquisitions, highlighting the potential for condition-dependent backscatter metric selection to improve SAR-based river WSE retrieval.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Limited labeled data and class imbalance complicate polarimetric synthetic aperture radar (PolSAR) image classification. We propose a complex-valued multiscale feature interaction attention vision transformer (CV-MfIaViT) that combines spatial analytic-envelope preprocessing, complex-valued local feature extraction and global modeling. A two-dimensional Hilbert transform (2D HT) provides envelope features to the shallow pathway. The complex-valued amplitude–phase interaction attention (CV-APIA) module generates positive real gains through cross-modulation of pooled features; the final gating preserves the element-wise phase of non-zero input features. A complex-valued feature complementary mapper (CV-FCM), a complex-valued multiscale extractor (CV-MSE) and a complex-valued vision transformer (CV-ViT) complete the architecture. Under random-pixel sampling, the reported average accuracy (AA) exceeds CV-MsAtViT by 1.83, 3.42 and 2.48 percentage points on Flevoland, San Francisco and Nanjing, respectively. The advantage is also retained under spatial-block partitioning with shared label-free scene-level normalization. Component ablations and comparisons with two simple learned front ends characterize the combined design. The results support the evaluated architecture without isolating phase preservation as the cause of its accuracy gains.

</details>

The exploratory provisional assessment found a wet bias of 0.110 m3 m−3, concentrated at very dry reference sites, with a pooled R of −0.038 but an anomaly R within sites of 0.259. Abstract (英文): Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (InSAR) is a satellite–based remote sensing technique capable of measuring surface displacement. However, InSAR measurements can be affected by temporal decorrelation, baseline geometry, and surface characteristics. Persistent scatterer (PS) and small baseline subset (SBAS) are two widely used multi-temporal InSAR techniques for addressing these limitations. Processing SAR acquisitions to derive deformation maps and displacement time series requires a series of complex steps. Many existing open-source and commercial software packages support PS or SBAS, while some support both methods, each with its own challenges and limitations. In this study, we presnet SNAPxtra, a Python-based InSAR automatic workflow to generate PS and SBAS interferometric stacks. These can be exported in the format required for time-series analysis in LiCSBAS and StaMPS. SNAPxtra has automated master selection, multi-swath and multi-frame processing, polygon-based masking, trial processing, and network generation features within an integrated workflow. The reproducibility and adaptability of SNAPxtra are demonstrated through case studies in Quetta (Pakistan), Surat (India), and Fort Worth (USA) as case studies using both PS and SBAS methods.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Radar precipitation information with high spatial and temporal resolution is essential for flood forecasting, urban pluvial flood warning, and digital twin basins, yet radar quantitative precipitation estimation (QPE) is affected by observational errors, retrieval relationships, terrain effects, and multi-source merging. Based on 1328 Web of Science Core Collection publications (2000–2025), this study integrates bibliometric analysis, Transformer-based semantic representation, BERTopic modeling, semantic association analysis, and bibliographic coupling to map the topic structure, thematic evolution, and methodological lineages of research on radar precipitation uncertainty and hydrological applications. Nineteen micro-level topics were identified and grouped into four categories: measurement error identification and correction, uncertainty characterization and modeling, error propagation and scale effects, and operational applications and risk decision-making. The semantic structure shows that observation and correction and hydrological propagation constitute the main branches, while AI-driven forecasting is emerging. Methodological lineage analysis indicates that Statistical Fusion dominates the field, Physics provides a foundation, and AI and Hybrid methods have grown markedly in recent years, with Hybrid methods bridging physical mechanisms, statistical fusion, and data-driven approaches. Future research should integrate explainable AI, physical constraints, probabilistic modeling, and hydrological-response consistency testing to advance chain-wide uncertainty representation from observation and forecasting to hydrological propagation and risk-informed decision-making.

Abstract (英文): The consequences of drought for rivers depend not only on the atmospheric water deficit but also on how catchments store and release water. It remains unclear how well catchment characteristics distinguish streamflow drought occurrence under similar meteorological conditions. We combined AlphaEarth satellite descriptors with principal component analysis to examine this question in 361 relatively undisturbed catchments across the conterminous United States. Using the three-month Standardized Precipitation Evapotranspiration Index (SPEI) and Standardized Streamflow Index (SSI), we linked meteorological droughts to subsequent streamflow droughts and assessed whether catchment landscapes helped explain differences in their occurrence, intensity and duration under comparable meteorological and initial streamflow conditions. Occurrence was most clearly associated with PC1, the dominant component of the AlphaEarth descriptors, whose landscape differences were partly reflected in drainage density and forest cover. At comparable meteorological drought durations and the same meteorological intensity and initial streamflow conditions, catchments in the upper PC1 quartile had estimated occurrence probabilities 8.1–20.9 percentage points below those in the lower quartile. The separation was greater for moderate-or-stronger drought, at 13.8–22.3 points. Higher PC1 also tended to accompany lower drought intensity, but the evidence for intensity and duration differences was less conclusive than for occurrence. These findings support the use of a common satellite-derived landscape description to identify catchments more susceptible to streamflow drought under comparable meteorological conditions, while showing that occurrence probability alone cannot indicate how severe or persistent the resulting drought will be.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Cross-sensor landslide mapping is limited by differences in image resolution and unreliable target pseudo-labels. We present SCALAR (Scale Anchoring and Label-Free Recalibration), an unsupervised adaptation workflow built on an exponential-moving-average teacher and ClassMix. GSD Anchoring (GSA) uses ground sampling distance metadata for bounded resampling. Logit-Otsu Calibration (LOC) jointly adjusts the pseudo-label boundary and relative retention margins. Ratio-Signed Regularization (RSR) makes these margins asymmetric according to the target-to-anchor GSD ratio. It is exploratory; GSA + LOC with symmetric margins is the recommended configuration. These modules introduce no trainable parameters. In the primary CAS region-held-out evaluation across five seeds, GSA + LOC increased the landslide intersection over union (IoU) by 17.9±4.5 points beyond scale anchoring when transferring satellite/aerial imagery (SAT) to unmanned aerial vehicle imagery (UAV), with no resolved additional gain in UAV-to-SAT. At seed 0, SAT-to-UAV threshold-independent average precision (AP) increased from 0.3484 for the anchored source to 0.4809 with GSA + LOC on held-out regions, compared with an increase from 0.6371 to 0.6495 under the original transductive protocol. Under that protocol, fixed-checkpoint region-cluster bootstrap supported GSA gains in both directions and LOC gains only in SAT-to-UAV; RSR’s 95% intervals included zero in both directions. UAV-to-SAT benefits varied across backbones, and negative transfer occurred in some tested settings. The findings support coordinating scale and pseudo-label decisions within CAS while identifying teacher reliability and target resolution as conditions to examine before broader deployment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Stratospheric ozone exhibits quasi-biennial-range variability, whose spatial organization can differ among pressure levels and longitudes. We analyzed Aqua Atmospheric Infrared Sounder (AIRS) Version 7 monthly ozone observations from September 2002 to December 2025 at 50, 10, and 3 hPa. A Morlet wavelet analysis of standardized anomalies characterized the 20–36-month variability, with AR(1) Monte Carlo significance tests and complementary multiple-testing and uncertainty assessments. The tropical 10 hPa band showed relatively enhanced normalized power and widespread longitudinal support: 326 of 359 eligible longitudes remained significant after a within-level Benjamini–Hochberg adjustment of band-level tests. In the global 10 hPa grid-cell analysis, 6792 of 62,191 eligible cells passed the corresponding global Benjamini–Hochberg adjustment. Conditional block-bootstrap comparisons indicated differences in some predefined longitude-sector contrasts, whereas the confidence intervals for the 10-minus-50 hPa longitude-power coefficient-of-variation difference included zero. Regressions using NOAA CPC and KIT/FU Berlin QBO wind records indicated longitude-sector heterogeneity in the 10 hPa ozone–QBO regression associations using the 50 hPa wind index. The ERA5 temperature and low-wavenumber geopotential height patterns provided meteorological context, although the assessed ozone–ERA5 spatial alignments did not pass the adopted spatial-null tests. These findings offer a pressure-dependent, longitude-resolved description of quasi-biennial-range ozone variability and selected QBO-associated statistical relationships, without establishing a unique dynamical or chemical attribution.

Abstract (英文): Data from terrestrial GNSS receivers have been combined with point clouds from aerial lidar to estimate the average attenuation coefficients of foliage in planted pine forests at GNSS frequencies. The absorption and scattering of radio signals due to the presence of vegetation can degrade signals from communications and satellite-based navigation systems, making accurate estimates of attenuation essential for predicting equipment performance. Given the potentially limited ability of lidar to penetrate dense foliage, a method was developed to estimate the average attenuation of signals based on distance travelled beneath the maximum height envelope of tree canopies. This was accomplished by using aerial lidar data to determine the outer surface of the forest canopy based on the maximum height in each horizontal bin. Each GNSS satellite detection vector was then analysed to determine the total length traversed inside of the canopy envelope. Application to regions of planted radiata pine in a managed forest outside Adelaide, South Australia, yielded average attenuation coefficient estimates for satellites with a 30–60° elevation angle of 0.36 dB m−1 for 10-year-old medium-height and 0.35 dB m−1 for 19-year-old tall trees. The demonstrated method and results provide site and vegetation-type-specific data to inform predictive models of GNSS receiver performance at specific locations.

Abstract (英文): Tropospheric phase delay is a major error source that limits the accuracy of Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (InSAR) deformation measurements, including those from the C- and X-band systems commonly examined in the studies reviewed here. Traditional correction methods, including external atmospheric models, spatiotemporal filtering, and phase–elevation empirical approaches, can partially mitigate atmospheric delays but often suffer from limited resolution, strong assumptions, or difficulties in separating atmospheric signals from complex surface deformation. Recent advances in deep learning provide new opportunities for InSAR tropospheric correction by learning nonlinear spatial and temporal relationships from interferometric observations and auxiliary information. This review provides a focused overview of deep learning-based approaches for correcting tropospheric phase delays in InSAR. Existing studies are organized according to their dominant modeling strategies, including convolutional neural network (CNN)-based spatial modeling, recurrent neural network (RNN)-based temporal modeling, generative adversarial network (GAN)-based generative learning, and other feedforward regression approaches. Key network-design strategies, training-label and dataset construction methods, and representative applications are discussed. Finally, current limitations and future research directions are discussed, emphasizing physical consistency, deformation preservation, cross-region generalization, and independent geodetic validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Smoke from the Canadian wildfires of August 2025 reached Cluj-Napoca, Romania, on 6–9 August as a thin layer at 5–8 km. Along the air mass trajectory, the HARP2 retrievals resolved the ageing of the smoke: an optical depth decaying from 0.9 to 0.2–0.3, a layer rising to 10 km and descending to 5 km, growth of the fine-mode radius, and a single-scattering albedo consistent with brown carbon loss. At the site, the lidar ratio and particle depolarisation ratio retrieved by HARP2 (52 sr, 0.042) agreed with the lidar’s derived lidar ratio and measured depolarisation (50 sr, 0.042), whereas the three satellite optical depths differed by 0.2 around an AERONET value of 0.1 and the NATALI typing networks returned three different classes. For this faint, aged plume, intensive polarimetric and vertical quantities could no longer be identified for the aerosol by its optical depth and Ångström exponent, which motivates testing an intensive-first pairing of satellite polarimetry with ground-based lidar on further episodes.

Abstract (英文): Historic hill towns require geometric information capable of relating urban morphology, construction features, and structural details within a common spatial and semantic framework. This paper presents a multi-sensor, multi-scale geomatics workflow for establishing an integrated geospatial information framework for the historic centre of Fiumefreddo Bruzio in Calabria, southern Italy. A control network of 38 points provided the spatial reference for UAV and terrestrial photogrammetry, six SLAM trajectories, and 270 static laser scans covering five buildings. Point clouds generated by Structure from Motion and range-based sensors were co-registered in the RDN2008/UTM zone 33N coordinate reference system and used to produce orthophotos, digital terrain and surface models, contour lines, textured meshes, and building-scale drawings. These products were organised within an interoperable geospatial information system based on persistent links between settlement-, aggregate-, building-, façade-, space-, and macroelement-level entities. The framework also supported structural characterisation and seismic vulnerability assessment at building and urban scales, linking building screening and potential local mechanisms to their spatial interpretation. The resulting survey-based digital model supports conservation documentation, seismic screening, and street-network analysis, while providing the foundation for future digital twin development rather than an operational implementation. The study offers a transferable acquisition-to-information workflow, preserving data provenance and metric quality for risk-informed management of historic settlements.

Abstract (英文): Deep-learning research on flood inundation mapping is driven largely by architectural innovation and single-run reporting. We ask whether the segmentation architecture or the input representation contributes more to accuracy in flood-event surface-water mapping. We introduce GeoHybridNet, a hybrid CNN–Transformer network, and compare it on the hand-labeled Sen1Floods11 benchmark with a U-Net, an Attention U-Net, a parameter-matched wide U-Net, and SegFormer under matched data and training. The four convolutional and hybrid models differ by at most 1.4 IoU points, and none are significantly different under event-level tests; a compact SegFormer trained from scratch is about 3.5 points lower. In factorial designs crossing three architectures with up to five input representations (five seeds each), input representation explains about 95% of the variance in water IoU and architecture about 1%. The dominant input factor is radiometric normalization: raw bands standardized per chip reach only 60% IoU and adding NDWI restores 76%, whereas the same bands standardized with training-set statistics reach 77.5% without any index. The pattern holds when permanent water is excluded. On three held-out flood events, accuracy drops by 24–26 IoU points for every model. On this benchmark, input preparation and honest evaluation matter more than architecture.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate forest-cover mapping in fragmented Mediterranean landscapes remains challenging because forests, croplands, grasslands, and sparsely vegetated surfaces often exhibit overlapping spectral responses. This study benchmarks ten supervised machine-learning classifiers for regional forest-cover mapping in the Beni Mellal-Khenifra region of Morocco, evaluates the contribution of spectral, textural, and topographic predictors, and assesses the consistency of the resulting forest map with the Hansen Global Forest Change product. A reference dataset of 2800 samples representing forest, crops/grass, bare ground, urban areas, and water was combined with an annual 2025 Sentinel-2 median composite, spectral indices, NDVI-derived gray-level co-occurrence matrix textures, and SRTM-derived elevation and slope. The samples were divided once into a stratified 70% training set and a 30% test set common to all classifiers. Redundancy, correlation (|r| > 0.80), and variance inflation factor (VIF < 10) screening, fitted on the training set only, reduced 56 candidate predictors to 16, and hyperparameters were tuned by five-fold grid-search cross-validation within the training set. On the common test set (n = 840), CatBoost, XGBoost, and the multilayer perceptron each achieved an overall accuracy of 95.95% (Cohen’s κ = 0.947), followed by Gradient Tree Boosting (95.83%); McNemar tests with Holm correction detected no significant differences among these four models. Spatial block cross-validation reduced overall accuracy by 0.3–2.7 percentage points, and CatBoost retained the highest spatially blocked accuracy (95.51% with 10 km blocks). SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP) identified slope, SATVI, NDWI, and the blue band as the dominant contributors to CatBoost forest predictions. The leading maps estimated 859,057–870,663 ha of forest. CatBoost captured 90.9% of the Hansen forest extent (≥30% canopy cover) but mapped a much larger forest area; blind interpretation of stratified samples showed that 90.5% of the CatBoost-only forest was woody vegetation, mainly open woodland and matorral. These findings show that boosting ensembles combined with a compact, non-redundant predictor set provide accurate and spatially robust forest-cover maps and that differences from global products largely reflect forest definitions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Civil Air Patrol (CAP) imagery, often the first post-disaster observation, is designed for 2D interpretation rather than multi-temporal volumetric measurement. We build post-storm digital surface and terrain models from uncontrolled CAP imagery with structure-from-motion in western North Carolina’s open Swannanoa Valley and forested Hickory Nut Gorge. Surfaces are co-registered to pre-event LiDAR using roads as pseudo ground control, corrected for terrain-correlated bias, and thresholded with a spatially variable, calibrated Level of Detection. Before bias correction, bare-earth surfaces match withheld post-event LiDAR on stable terrain to 0.68 and 0.82 m normalized median absolute deviation. Volumes are 2017-to-2024 change, lower bounds in coverage, and conditional on the bias model, residual screen, and detection window. Corrected valley surfaces record 125,000 m3 of erosion and 123,000 m3 of deposition, and a sampled image check dates about 13% of that change to the sortie. Under the same limit, the uncorrected surface reports five times the deposition, and the alternative bias model gives gross terms 3.6 to 4.2 times larger. The gorge at Chimney Rock recorded 76,000 m3 of erosion and 84,000 m3 of positive change, just under half dating to the sortie, including land deposition partly cleared before the post-event LiDAR flew. CAP-derived surfaces can capture transient storage that later surveys cannot.

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral anomaly detection (HAD) requires effective background modeling while limiting the reconstruction of rare anomalous spectra. Conventional non-negative matrix factorization provides an interpretable endmember–abundance representation but offers limited flexibility for estimating background representations under complex spectral variability, whereas reconstruction-oriented autoencoders provide flexible nonlinear inference but often lack an explicit mixing-model structure. To bridge these two paradigms, we propose a Non-negative Latent Factorization Autoencoder (NLFA) for anomaly-oriented background modeling. NLFA employs a nonlinear encoder to estimate simplex-constrained abundance-like latent coefficients and a linear mixing model (LMM)-consistent linear decoder to represent the dominant background through endmember-like spectral bases. A column-sparse residual is jointly optimized with the latent factorization so that spectral components insufficiently explained by the learned background are retained for anomaly detection, while local spatial smoothness and endmember-diversity constraints regularize the background representation. Experiments on eight benchmark hyperspectral datasets show that NLFA achieves competitive detection performance.

</details>

Residual bias increased with canopy height and became more negative on steep slopes, where RMSE also increased. These results show that the proposed approach can generate continuous forest DTMs while quantifying the contributions of the main predictors to the offset, their variation across environmental conditions, and the conditions under which residual errors increase.

Abstract (英文): Huanglongbing (HLB) is the most destructive disease affecting citrus production worldwide, causing severe yield losses and tree decline. Hyperspectral proximal sensing provides detailed information on leaf reflectance; however, its effectiveness for reliable HLB detection depends on the identification of consistent and discriminative spectral patterns. In this study, hyperspectral reflectance data (400–2450 nm) were combined with supervised machine learning algorithms to distinguish HLB-positive and HLB-negative citrus samples at two complementary scales: (i) leaf-level classification using individual spectral signatures and (ii) plant-level classification based on aggregated spectral responses. Model performance was evaluated under a leave-one-field-out validation framework designed to minimize spatial dependence between training and testing data. Results demonstrated consistently high classification performance, with Logistic Regression achieving accuracies above 0.94 at the leaf level and up to 1.00 at the plant level in several configurations. Dimensionality reduction using Principal Component Analysis (PCA) preserved most of the discriminative information, although with a slight reduction in recall under certain FIELD combinations. In contrast, Class balancing produced no observed improvement, whereas hyperparameter optimization showed limited and configuration-dependent effects in classification performance. Spectral coefficient analysis revealed that the most relevant wavelengths are concentrated in the visible (400–700 nm), red-edge (700–750 nm), near-infrared (750–1300 nm), and shortwave infrared (1500–2500 nm) regions, reflecting physiological alterations associated with pigment degradation, mesophyll disruption, and changes in water and biochemical composition. Overall, hyperspectral reflectance combined with parsimonious machine learning models enabled consistent discrimination of HLB-positive and HLB-negative samples within the evaluated orchard under validation designed to reduce spatial dependence, particularly at the plant scale.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Image registration is fundamental to UAV remote sensing data processing, but repetitive textures, viewpoint and scale variations, terrain relief, and local deformation can introduce numerous outliers into tentative correspondences. The existing random-sampling and spatial-consistency methods commonly rely on pointwise confidence or isolated pairwise relations and therefore cannot reliably determine whether a locally compatible relation receives stable group-level structural support. To address this limitation, we propose SPGSC++, a second-order spatial guided sampling method for outlier removal. SPGSC++ first uses PCA to reduce the dimensionality of tentative correspondences, thereby reducing the computational burden of subsequent spatial relation construction. It then derives second-order structural quality from the common-neighbor support of directly compatible edges and uses this quality for initial data subset selection and residual-informed data subset refinement to generate and optimize a homography. Comparative experiments show that, on the five datasets CIAP, SUIRD, UAV, ZHANGZHOU, and PAN, the proposed method achieves mean Precision, Recall, F-Score, Runtime, and mean reprojection error values of 0.9627, 0.9797, 0.9702, 0.3724 s, and 1.6454, respectively. Relative to the original SPGSC, SPGSC++ increases the mean F-Score by 0.0391 and reduces Runtime and mean reprojection error by 32.39% and 58.12%, respectively. Overall, the innovation of SPGSC++ lies in using second-order common-neighbor structural quality to guide initial subset selection and residual-informed refinement for robust estimation in UAV remote sensing image registration.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The Mamba network has recently emerged as a powerful architecture for remote sensing change detection (RSCD). Notably, the fusion of multi-scale features and heterogeneous attention mechanisms has proven effective in enhancing the performance of Mamba network. However, existing methods fail to quantitatively evaluate the actual contribution weights of different components during feature fusion, thereby limiting the interpretability of their improvements. To address this issue, this paper proposes a novel mixture-of-scale-and-attention-experts Mamba Network (MoEMamba) for the RSCD task, which has the following innovations and contributions: (1) a Siamese Mamba architecture is employed to extract features from bi-temporal images; (2) a mixture-of-scale-experts module is designed to enhance the generalization ability across multi-scale objects, which quantitatively evaluates the contribution of multiple scale-specific experts; and (3) a mixture-of-attention-experts module is proposed to improve the accuracy, which adaptively fuses complementary responses of heterogeneous attention mechanisms based on the learned weights. Extensive experiments on the WHU-CD and LEVIR-CD datasets demonstrate that MoEMamba outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods. Specifically, it achieves F1 scores of 94.20% and 92.62%, and IoU values of 89.04% and 86.25%, respectively. Importantly, this study provides valuable insights into the interpretability of multi-scale feature fusion and heterogeneous attention fusion for RSCD research.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fine-scale vegetation mapping supports habitat management, landscape change monitoring, and local planning. Segmentation was performed using Geographic Object-Based Image Analysis (GEOBIA) at two hierarchical levels with 0.6 m National Agriculture Imagery Program (NAIP) imagery and a LiDAR-derived digital height model. Training and validation data were obtained from a legacy ecological plot database originally collected for purposes other than classification. Of the 34 classes initially targeted for mapping, 27 were ultimately mapped after spectrally inseparable classes were collapsed into coarser hierarchical levels. Mean overall accuracy across 1000 training and validation partitions was 54.8%, with a minimum of 51.2% to a maximum of 65.6%. Phenological variables derived from PlanetScope imagery improved overall accuracy by an average of 6.5 percentage points. Overall classification accuracy varied by 12.7 percentage points across partitions, indicating that overall accuracy estimates based on a single training and validation split may provide a poor representation of expected model performance.

Abstract (英文): A key correction in altimetry measurements is the wet tropospheric correction (WTC), which accounts for the delay on the measured range predominantly caused by water vapor in the troposphere. The WTC is currently one of the main sources of error affecting the altimeter range over ocean surfaces, with direct impact on estimates of sea surface height and the global mean sea level. Most current altimetry missions rely on onboard passive microwave radiometers that measure the brightness temperature at key frequencies for the spatio-temporal collocated estimation of the WTC. For such missions, the current operational algorithms for the estimation of the WTC can be placed in two categories: those using a neural network and those using a log-linear equation dependent on classes of WTC and windspeed. These two methodologies both follow a semi-empirical approach, but differ significantly in the inputs used, the training databases on which they are developed, and the mathematical approach itself. The objective of this study is to conduct a comprehensive comparison between these two algorithms, focusing solely on the mathematical aspect, in order to determine if the differences in the approaches of the two algorithms have a significant effect on the resulting WTC products, their performance and their limitations. To ensure the identical conditions required to achieve this, we develop the two algorithms so that they take the same inputs, have the same training database, and then we apply them to identical simulated and observed datasets to determine their performance. We use the three-frequency configuration (18.7 GHz−23.8 GHz−34 GHz) of the Advanced Microwave Radiometer for Climate (AMR-C) on Copernicus Sentinel-6 Michael Freilich (Sentinel-6MF), and radiative transfer modelling using the Radiative Transfer for TOVS (RTTOV), to create a simulated database that will serve for both development of the algorithms and performance evaluation. Both algorithms are then applied to the same observations from Copernicus Sentinel-6MF to characterise their observational performance in comparison to both model and operational products, as well as to each other. We find that, despite the significant differences in the two methodologies, when the algorithms are developed and applied to the same databases, they deliver very similar performances both on simulated and observed datasets and face similar limitations. In addition, for both algorithms we find performances similar to or slightly better than that of the operational WTC product for Copernicus Sentinel-6MF.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Ground surface subsidence resulting from underground mining is characterized by significant spatial deformation gradients and nonlinear temporal deformation. A single monitoring technique is insufficient to simultaneously achieve extensive spatial coverage and high temporal continuity. Interferometric Synthetic Aperture Radar (InSAR) provides high spatial resolution but is limited by sparse temporal sampling, whereas the Global Navigation Satellite System (GNSS) offers dense temporal sampling but with restricted spatial coverage. To address these limitations, this study introduces a spatio-temporal fusion method combining InSAR and GNSS, based on baseline deformation field–residual decoupling (BR-STKF), specifically applied to the Banji Coal Mine in the Huainan Mining Area, Anhui Province. Sentinel-1 SBAS-InSAR is employed to derive Line-Of-Sight (LOS) deformation time series, while three-dimensional GNSS displacements are projected onto the LOS direction. A daily scale baseline deformation field is constructed using pixel-wise Kalman filtering and Rauch–Tung–Striebel (RTS) smoothing. Subsequently, residual signals between the InSAR/GNSS observations and the baseline field are estimated utilizing a correlation-anchored multiscale spatial basis and an information-form Kalman filter. Helmert variance component estimation is introduced to adaptively adjust the weights of InSAR and GNSS observations. To demonstrate the reliability of the proposed framework, a quantitative validation was conducted. The experimental results yielded root mean square error (RMSE) values of 6.37 mm for strict spatial leave-one-out cross-validation (LOOCV) and 2.84 mm for retrospective reconstruction with missing InSAR phases. These results are competitive and comparable to leading benchmarks such as the Spatio-Temporal Kalman Filter (STKF), while achieving lower mean RMSEs than baseline methods such as Spatio-Temporal Random Effects (STREs), Enhanced Spatio-Temporal Random Effects (eSTREs), Improved Spatio-Temporal Random Effects (ISTREs), Multi-resolution Orthogonal Polynomial Basis (MOP), and ST-Kriging. However, the statistical stability of differences among models requires further verification with additional independent data. The findings suggest that the baseline deformation field–residual decoupling strategy effectively integrates the spatial coverage of InSAR with daily GNSS observations, offering a viable framework for daily scale line-of-sight (LOS) deformation reconstruction in mining areas.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Compound temperature and soil moisture extremes threaten terrestrial carbon uptake, but their contrasting ecological impacts remain poorly understood. We assessed dry–hot (DH), wet–hot (WH), dry–cold (DC), and wet–cold (WC) events across China from 2001 to 2025. The analyses combined daily air temperature, root-zone soil moisture, and MODIS-derived gross primary productivity (GPP). We quantified event occurrence, negative-response probability, cumulative GPP loss, and recovery time, and examined environmental predictors using explainable machine learning. WC events were most frequent, whereas DH events lasted longest. WH frequency increased over the study period, while DC frequency declined. DH and DC produced negative GPP responses in 63.5% and 67.1% of classifiable events, respectively. Among negative-response events, DH had the largest national mean cumulative event-period GPP loss below the −0.5 SD threshold (2.85 g C m−2) and the longest conditional recovery time (3.17 days). WC losses exceeded DC losses despite a lower negative-response probability. Losses varied with elevation, season, and event severity. Vapor pressure deficit dominated predictions of DH negative responses and losses, whereas other event types showed greater contributions from radiation, precipitation, and soil texture. These findings highlight the need to distinguish event occurrence, response probability, productivity loss, and recovery when assessing ecological risks from compound extremes.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Diameter at breast height (DBH) and tree height are core parameters for forest monitoring. LiDAR enables rapid high-precision point cloud acquisition, yet single-platform data have limitations: airborne laser scanning (ALS) cannot capture sub-canopy structure, while terrestrial laser scanning (TLS) cannot fully acquire canopy information. Based on 92 plots in Guangxi, this study proposes a weighted point-to-normal iterative closest point (WPN-ICP) registration algorithm for high-precision fusion of ALS and TLS point clouds, with comparisons to trimmed iterative closest point (TrICP) and iterative closest point (ICP). An adaptive density-based spatial clustering of applications with noise (DBSCAN) algorithm was developed for diameter at breast height (DBH) estimation, and a DBH cluster-based vertical layering method (DCVL-THE) for tree height estimation. Three segmentation-based methods—canopy height model (CHM), region growing (RG), and comparative shortest path (CSP)—were selected for comparison. WPN-ICP achieved a mean MAE of 0.0359 m, with reductions of 85.87%, 69.03%, and 73.00% compared with coarse registration, TrICP, and ICP. Using the fused point cloud, DBH estimation achieved an RMSE of 0.6326 cm with an RRMSE of 5.18%, and tree height estimation achieved an RMSE of 0.7160 m with an RRMSE of 6.58%. Compared with the suboptimal CSP method, the RMSE and RRMSE for DBH were reduced by 41.79% and 41.68%, and for tree height by 25.66% and 25.68%. This method does not require full individual-tree or crown segmentation, thereby avoiding the error propagation associated with such segmentation and enabling accurate stand-scale estimation.

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral image (HSI) classification is a fundamental remote sensing task, but the high-dimensional nature of HSI data poses challenges for acquisition, storage, transmission, and processing. To mitigate these issues, researchers have developed compressive classification methods based on the coded aperture snapshot spectral imaging (CASSI) system, enabling direct classification in the compressed domain without reconstructing the full hyperspectral cube. However, existing methods often fail to fully leverage spatial-spectral correlations, suffer from limited multi-scale feature extraction, and are constrained by fixed receptive fields and degradation caused by compression. To overcome these limitations, we propose ECSA-UNet, an end-to-end framework that directly classifies compressive spectral measurements from CASSI. The model introduces a spatial-spectral decoupling module and refines the U-Net architecture with global–local feature extraction, Spatial-Spectral Attention, a Multi-Scale Enhancement Pyramid and Cross-Stage Connections, enabling dynamic receptive fields, improved long-range dependency modeling, and robust local–global feature fusion. Moreover, the CASSI coded aperture is parameterized as a trainable component, allowing for joint optimization of coding and classification within an end-to-end learning paradigm. Extensive experiments on four benchmarks show ECSA-UNet achieves state-of-the-art accuracy, and ablations confirm each module’s contribution. In addition, it maintains an effective trade-off between parameter overhead and computational efficiency, offering a viable solution for latency-sensitive hyperspectral remote sensing tasks.

</details>

Abstract (英文): To address scale-like errors in local point clouds caused by pitch angle errors in unmanned aerial vehicle-borne LiDAR bathymetry (UAV-ALB) systems, this study proposes a correction method based on scanline keypoint constraints. First, the point cloud is traversed using scanline information to extract geometric keypoint features from each scanline. Next, a scale-like error correction model is constructed based on the distance differences between keypoints at the start and end of overlapping scanlines, and the pitch angle correction value is estimated using a forward–backward fusion approach. The estimated correction is then applied to the attitude data, and the point cloud is reprocessed. This procedure is iterated until the convergence criterion is satisfied. Experimental results show that, after correction, the horizontal root mean square errors (RMSE) of the two datasets decrease from 0.217 and 0.283 m to 0.025 and 0.030 m, respectively, while the elevation RMSE values decrease from 0.227 and 0.241 m to 0.006 and 0.004 m, respectively. These results demonstrate that the proposed method effectively mitigates scale-like errors in UAV-ALB point clouds and substantially improves their geometric consistency and elevation accuracy.

Abstract (英文): Forest canopy height is a key parameter for estimating forest biomass and carbon stocks, as well as a fundamental indicator for assessing forest ecosystem health. Although existing 10 m resolution global canopy height products are free and open-source, they suffer from substantial systematic errors due to the inherent biases of spaceborne LiDAR and the nonlinear relationship between optical imagery and canopy height, making them insufficiently accurate as direct training labels for deep learning. This study proposes a complete framework of “adaptive stratified screening, followed by weakly supervised UNet training and minimal ALS post-processing refinement.” First, using the 12 spectral bands of Sentinel-2 multispectral imagery, the OOB distance is calculated via weighted random forest as an anomaly metric. Through systematic comparison of three anomaly metrics (Cook’s distance, OOB distance, and standardized residuals) and five retention ratios (30–50%), the OOB distance at 40% retention is identified as optimal. An adaptive retention strategy based on tree height stratification is then implemented: for low-tree areas (<20 m), 40% of low-anomaly samples are uniformly retained; for tall-tree areas (≥20 m), the retention ratio is dynamically adjusted according to the proportion of tall-tree pixels (95% when tall-tree proportion < 10%, 70% when 10–30%, and 50% when ≥30%). The screened high-confidence Lang data are then used as supervisory signals to train a UNet model for predicting canopy height across the entire region, producing an initial canopy height map (MAE = 6.97 m, Bias = +4.21 m). Finally, using only a minimal amount of airborne LiDAR data (down to 0.1% area proportion), with a 14-dimensional input comprising the initial predicted height, screening mask values, and the 12 spectral bands of Sentinel-2, a multilayer perceptron is independently trained for each region to perform post-processing refinement of the initial predictions. Experiments based on 68 independent regions from the Open-Canopy dataset, with a strict regional split (34 validation/34 test), show that post-processing refinement reduces the test set MAE from 6.97 m (ASS) to 3.29 m (0.1% ALS, a 52.8% improvement, 58.1% improvement over the original Lang product), Bias from +4.21 m to −0.13 m, and RMSE from 8.95 m to 4.72 m. Even when the ALS proportion is reduced to 0.01%, the MAE remains 3.57 m (a 48.8% improvement). Statistical significance testing (paired Wilcoxon signed-rank test, n = 34) indicates that ASS+MLP(0.1%) significantly outperforms ASS (p < 0.00001). This study provides an effective technical pathway for regional refinement of global canopy height products.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The HY-2B/C/D constellation delivers routine altimeter significant wave height (SWH) and sea-surface wind speed over the Chinese coastal seas through four product streams (GDR, IDR, ODR, and SDR), yet whether the three satellites are mutually consistent, and whether that consistency is the same for both parameters, has not been established. We assess three years (2023–2025) of 1 Hz Ku-band retrievals against 30 quality-controlled coastal buoys, using a deduplicated matchup database, a common-buoy and common-period protocol with bootstrap confidence intervals, and triple collocation against ERA5. Within each satellite, the product streams are consistent for both parameters: the reprocessed GDR shifts the operational SWH by a sea-state-independent −0.157 m and the wind streams coincide to rounding. Across satellites, consistency is parameter-dependent. For SWH, the biases on common buoys span 0.18 m; HY-2B has the lowest random error, significantly lower than HY-2D, and HY-2C cannot be separated from either in random error. For wind speed, HY-2C reads about 1 m s−1 low in all four Chinese seas and in every wind band once the bands are defined by an independent reference, so that most of the offset is removable by a constant. The near-shore degradation of SWH inside 50 km largely reflects the sub-0.5 m sea states that dominate the near-shore buoys, and wind speed shows no comparable coastal signal. We translate these findings into applicability bounds and a parameter-aware recipe for merging the constellation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Unmanned aerial vehicle (UAV) thermal infrared remote sensing can rapidly acquire thermal-response information for large-scale photovoltaic (PV) arrays. Nevertheless, local thermal anomalies occupy small regions within aerial images; their features tend to degrade during successive down-sampling. Moreover, model deployment on edge devices is constrained by limited computational and memory resources. To address these challenges, this paper proposes TP-YOLO, a photovoltaic anomaly-detection approach, and implements it on the Zynq-7020 hardware platform. First, a three-channel thermal-prior input consisting of luminance distribution, local luminance difference, and a luminance gradient is constructed from pseudo-color infrared images, and explicitly characterizes thermal anomalies without requiring absolute-temperature measurements. Next, the detection scales of YOLOv11n are adjusted to P2/P3/P4. Modules including A-C3k2, Conv + Add, and TLCB are adopted to optimize feature extraction and multi-scale fusion, preserving local anomaly features while restraining model complexity. On the hardware side, a PS/PL co-processing architecture is employed. A dedicated convolution IP, ADD IP, and Window IP are designed for convolution, element-wise fusion, and local-window operations, respectively. INT8 quantization and ping-pong buffering are further utilized to realize network deployment. On the PV HSD test set, TP-YOLO achieves an mAP@0.5 of 77.5% and an mAP@0.5:0.95 of 33.7%, with 1.91 M parameters and 6.2 GFLOPs computational cost. In cross-dataset evaluation on the Drone Dataset without fine-tuning, the mAP@0.5 reaches 64.4%. After deploying the INT8 quantized model onto Zynq-7020, the implemented system yields an mAP@0.5 of 76.8%, achieving single-frame inference latency of 162 ms and throughput of 6.17 FPS at 150 MHz, with a power consumption of 3.4 W.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Safe and reliable carbon capture and storage (CCS) requires long-term monitoring to verify secure CO2 containment. Distributed acoustic sensing vertical seismic profiling (DAS-VSP) provides a practical approach for subsurface monitoring due to its permanent installation, dense spatial sampling, and reduced operational costs. However, monitoring programs spanning decades may require hardware upgrades, including interrogator replacements and changes to fibre-optic cable configurations, affecting time-lapse repeatability and interpretability. This field case study evaluates whether the CO2-induced 4D DAS-VSP response remains interpretable across a combined change in interrogator, fibre type and optical path, whose individual contributions cannot be isolated. The assessment uses data from the CO2CRC Otway Project Stage 4, where approximately 10 kilotons of CO2-rich reservoir gas were injected into a saline aquifer at approximately 1500 m depth. Baseline and monitor surveys using different DAS configurations are compared at two monitoring wells, with a same-configuration case as a repeatability benchmark. An established 4D DAS-VSP processing workflow, supplemented by system-specific noise attenuation, is applied to mitigate configuration-dependent differences. The results show that, despite higher non-repeatability, the principal CO2-related time-lapse anomaly remains spatially localised and distinguishable, indicating that the DAS-VSP response can remain interpretable across a combined configuration change under favourable acquisition and signal-quality conditions.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Long-term monitoring of wetland degradation, recovery, and spatial resilience requires methods that can jointly exploit multi-temporal remote sensing observations and spatially heterogeneous environmental information. This study develops an intelligent remote sensing framework for monitoring the disturbance–recovery dynamics and spatial resilience of typical wetland landscapes in Northeast China by coupling Landsat-based Detection of Trends in Disturbance and Recovery (LandTrendr) with geographically weighted random forest (GWRF). Annual wetland landscape state sequences for 2000–2022 are constructed from multi-temporal Landsat observations and associated geospatial variables. LandTrendr time-series segmentation is used to identify disturbance timing, disturbance magnitude, recovery rate, and recovery duration, thereby converting long-term wetland change trajectories into quantitative indicators of degradation and recovery. At the GIS grid scale, remotely sensed vegetation and wetness indicators are integrated with wetland patch structure, hydrological conditions, topography, land-cover change, and anthropogenic pressure. GWRF then models local nonlinear relationships under spatial-distance weighting to generate a spatially explicit wetland resilience index and reveal geographically varying dominant controls. The LandTrendr–GWRF model achieved an R2 of 0.872, an RMSE of 0.057, and an MAE of 0.039, outperforming RF, GWR, XGBoost, and standalone GWRF benchmarks. The mean wetland resilience index was 0.641, with low-, medium-, and high-resilience areas accounting for 27.86%, 44.37%, and 27.77% of the study area, respectively. Significant spatial clustering was detected (Moran’s I = 0.421, Z = 6.92, p = 0.001). Hydrological regulation, landscape connectivity, fragmentation constraint, and anthropogenic disturbance pressure emerged as the four dominant spatial response types. High-resilience cells formed contiguous clusters where hydrological recharge and landscape connectivity were strong, while low-resilience cells clustered where fragmentation and construction pressure were concentrated. Disturbance magnitude and recovery duration differed markedly among the four response types, with connectivity-dominated cells returning to the pre-disturbance state within 4.4 years and anthropogenic pressure-dominated cells requiring 11.7 years. The proposed framework extends wetland remote sensing from static condition mapping toward process-oriented monitoring of degradation, recovery, connectivity, and resilience, providing a practical approach for long-term wetland restoration assessment and spatial management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Aboveground biomass (AGB) is considered a key variable in surveilling the sustainability of forest management and the carbon cycle. This study examined the effects of image acquisition season and topographic variables on the performance of AGB estimates using Sentinel-2 imagery from three seasons. The study was conducted in 224 sample plots in pure Calabrian pine (Pinus brutia) stands in southern Türkiye. Random forest models were trained on 26 datasets derived from rainy, arid, and semi-arid season imagery, including single-season, two-season, and three-season combinations with and without topographic variables. Models trained using satellite images from rainy and semi-arid seasons performed better than models trained using data from the more arid season. All two-season combinations outperformed the corresponding single-season models, but none reached the accuracy of the three-season model. Including topographic variables reduced RMSE for every season and season combination. The best results were obtained using all three seasons together with topographic variables (R2 = 0.74, RMSE = 13.97 Mg ha−1), and removing topography from this model increased RMSE to 15.69 Mg ha−1. According to the SHAP analysis, elevation was the second-most influential predictor after Aug_SAVI (mean SHAP value = 1.65 Mg ha−1) and the only topographic variable that notably contributed to predictive performance. These findings indicate that, for the pure Calabrian pine stands and conditions examined in this study, combining multi-seasonal Sentinel-2 imagery with topographic variables improves AGB estimation accuracy compared with single-season models and spectral data alone.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Methane is a major greenhouse gas that plays a critical role in climate change. Accurate monitoring of methane at the regional scale is essential for understanding its spatiotemporal dynamics and underlying processes. Here, a machine learning-based framework is developed to retrieve column-averaged methane (XCH4) by integrating shortwave infrared spectral information from TROPOMI. Spectral channels are first analyzed through machine learning-based feature importance analysis, and normalized spectral features are subsequently constructed. These features are then combined with auxiliary variables, including meteorological conditions, observation geometry, and surface environmental factors, to develop an XCH4 retrieval model. The GOSAT L2 XCH4 product is introduced as a high-accuracy reference observation for correcting the retrieved XCH4 results, and independent validation is conducted using observations from the TCCON site. Among the evaluated models, XGBoost demonstrates the best performance, achieving an R2 of 0.91 and an RMSE of 9.28 ppb under five-fold cross-validation. The model also maintains relatively better predictive performance compared with other models under both temporal and spatial extrapolation scenarios. Compared with the TROPOMI L2 product, the retrieved XCH4 shows improved agreement with TCCON observations, with a higher correlation (R = 0.84) and reduced errors. Based on this approach, a daily, spatially continuous XCH4 dataset over China for 2021 is generated. Analysis of the retrieved dataset reveals pronounced spatiotemporal patterns, characterized by higher XCH4 concentrations in eastern China and lower values in the western regions, as well as a distinct seasonal cycle.

Abstract (英文): Landslides are among the most serious geohazards worldwide, and accurately identifying their locations is a prerequisite for effective risk prevention and mitigation. However, regional-scale screening of candidate active-slope units from InSAR observations is still hindered by subjective manual interpretation, inconsistent deformation-rate thresholds, and insufficient topographic constraints. To address these limitations, this study proposes a largely automated terrain-constrained framework for candidate active-slope screening by integrating InSAR observations, slope units, and screening based on slope-unit bootstrap intervals and a regional two-standard-deviation background envelope. First, surface deformation rates are derived from InSAR measurements and post-processed to reduce the influence of identifiable artifacts before slope-unit analysis. Then, a clustering-based method is introduced to delineate topographically constrained slope units in alpine canyon regions, whose visual terrain correspondence is examined qualitatively using terrain information and optical imagery. Next, rather than applying a fixed site-specific deformation-rate cutoff in mm/yr, data-dependent bootstrap confidence-interval comparisons are used to determine whether the deformation interval of each slope unit extends beyond the regional deformation-background interval, thereby enabling rule-based screening of candidate active-slope units. This confidence-interval screening rule provides a quantitative criterion for regional screening but requires further calibration across additional geomorphic and deformation settings. Finally, the proposed method is applied to two landslide-prone regions in western China, located in sections of the Upper Yellow River and Upper Jinsha River, where final inventories containing 92 and 25 candidate active-slope units are produced, respectively. These polygons are geomorphologically constrained candidate active-slope units produced for regional screening and should not be interpreted as the exact boundaries of individual landslides. Inventory-based spatial comparison and targeted optical inspection were reported as complementary evidence streams rather than as independent validation of contemporaneous slope activity. These results document implementation in two related alpine river-valley settings in western China and indicate the framework’s potential as a quantitatively defined, topographically constrained approach for largely automated regional screening of candidate active-slope units in comparable settings. These two applications do not establish broad applicability across substantially different environments.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Recent blind hyperspectral image unmixing methods have achieved effective spectral–spatial representation, yet the reliability of estimated abundances remains difficult to assess because errors in jointly estimated endmembers and abundances can compensate for each other. To address this issue, we propose Residual Diagnosis-Guided Abundance Correction (RDAC), a post-correction framework that preserves the initial abundance estimate as an anchor and characterizes its mismatch using structured reconstruction residuals. Abundance candidates are then combined with structured residual information, while only 1% reference abundances are used to learn the correction. RDAC was evaluated on the Jasper Ridge, Samson, and Urban datasets. With UNMamba as the primary blind-unmixing backbone, RDAC achieved A-RMSEs of 0.0229, 0.0175, and 0.0359, reducing the initial errors by 55.05%, 70.80%, and 59.63%, respectively. It also achieved the lowest mean A-RMSE among the compared methods on Jasper Ridge and Samson while remaining competitive on Urban. Further experiments showed that reconstruction error alone does not reliably indicate abundance accuracy. When applied to NMF, RDAC reduced A-RMSE by 80.45%, 89.25%, and 77.61% on the three datasets. The results indicate that structured residual diagnosis can support abundance correction under limited supervision, and the NMF experiment shows that the same correction framework can also be used with an alternative blind-unmixing backbone.

Abstract (英文): The characteristics and formation mechanisms of lightning activity during the lifetime of Super Typhoon Doksuri (2023) were investigated using ERA5 reanalysis data and observation data from the World-Wide Lightning Location Network (WWLLN) and Himawari-8/9 satellite. The lightning activity exhibited pronounced intermittent outbreak behavior, and intense lightning flashes mainly occurred in the outer rainband region approximately 200–600 km from the typhoon center. The occurrence of intense lightning was mainly related to the development of deep convections which were characterized by a high CAPE index and low cloud-top brightness temperature. In the landfall stage, intense lightning outbreaks may have been further modulated by the orographic lifting from the underlying surface and island thermal effects. Strong updrafts within the typhoon provided favorable conditions for intracloud microphysical electrification processes. The formation of intense lightning was interpreted via the non-inductive charging mechanism and validated using E-WRF model simulations. During the first rapid-intensification episode, intense inner-core lightning preceded the typhoon intensity peak by 14–41 h; during the re-intensification episode, it occurred ~9 h after the typhoon peak. These insights into the intense lightning activity in Super Typhoon Doksuri (2023) contribute to improving typhoon forecasting and disaster warning in the Western Pacific.

</details>

We use SMAP-R hybrid compact polarimetric observations and ERA5 wind and wave data to assess whether polarimetric features improve sea surface wind speed retrieval. With total significant wave height included in every baseline, paired non-polarimetric and polarimetric configurations are evaluated on identical samples using a geophysical model function with minimum-variance fusion (GMF/MV), extreme gradient boosting (XGBoost), and a fully connected network trained by back-propagation (BP-FCN). Adding the degree of polarization and normalized third Stokes component reduces the overall root mean square error (RMSE) by 7.83%, 5.49%, and 5.31%, respectively, with the polarimetric BP-FCN configuration achieving the lowest RMSE of 1.666 m s−1. Wind speed-stratified analysis shows that the relative error reduction from polarimetric information varies non-monotonically across wind speed intervals. Across all signal-to-noise ratio (SNR) intervals represented in this study, all three methods achieve positive polarimetric gains, with paired month-block bootstrap 95% intervals lying above zero, indicating benefits across different SNR conditions. Under low-wind, high-swell conditions, RMSE decreases by 10.58%, 11.26%, and 9.82%, respectively, exceeding the overall relative gains. Error decomposition indicates that these improvements mainly reflect reduced systematic overestimation. Comparisons against ASCAT scatterometer and NDBC buoy winds using all collocated samples also show positive polarimetric gains of 4.83–5.35% and 4.80–5.78%, respectively. These results demonstrate that polarimetric observations can further improve GNSS-R wind speed retrieval accuracy beyond the information already included in the baselines, with more pronounced gains under the low-wind, high-swell conditions sampled in this study, providing useful complementary information for sea surface wind speed retrieval under complex sea states.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Drought affects meteorological forcing, root-zone water availability and land-surface functioning at different spatial and temporal supports. We evaluated Uruguay’s 2020–2023 drought using an observational 2016–2024 panel of 1423 SMAP parent cells. Monthly SPEI-3, SPEI-6 and SPEI-12 represented short, intermediate and persistent climatic water-balance deficits. SMAP root-zone soil moisture (RZSM) was retained at 9 km support, whereas actual evapotranspiration (ETa) and the moisture stress index (MSI) were summarized from approximately 500 m pixels using annual MapBiomas fractions for grassland, cropland and silviculture within each parent cell. For each cell and 8-day composite position, responses were expressed relative to the corresponding 2016–2019 mean. An interval was classified as locally dry when its overlap-day-weighted SPEI-12 was below −1. During 2020–2023, RZSM responses ranged from −13.09% to −16.63% among dominant landscape domains. Fraction-weighted ETa responses were −8.69% for grassland, −6.75% for cropland and −7.39% for silviculture, while oriented MSI responses were −6.62%, −3.32% and −11.20%, respectively. At lag zero, monthly RZSM correlated the most strongly with SPEI-3 (r = 0.81), followed by SPEI-6 (r = 0.64) and SPEI-12 (r = 0.51). At native 8-day support, RZSM was positively aligned with ETa (r = 0.42–0.57) and the oriented MSI (r = 0.31–0.59), although prewhitening weakened some associations. Overall, the 2020–2023 drought was consistently expressed across meteorological, root-zone and land-surface indicators. The strong association between SPEI-3 and RZSM, together with consistent land-surface departures and product-dependent ETa variation among components, highlights the value of multiscale monitoring for characterizing drought intensity and ecosystem responses across Uruguay.

Abstract (英文): Understanding and estimating the impact of atmospheric turbulence on the growth of precipitation particles in freezing weather can help improve the prediction of freezing rain precipitation. In this study, the turbulent kinetic energy dissipation rate (ε) during a freezing weather event was retrieved using the power spectral analysis method, based on observational data from a Ka-band millimeter-wave cloud radar, radiosondes, and ground automatic weather stations, and the influence of atmospheric turbulence on the growth of freezing rain precipitation particles was investigated using nonlinear causal methods such as transfer entropy (TE), convergent cross-mapping (CCM), the phase-randomized surrogate test, and partial CCM. Causality tests confirmed a significant bidirectional causal relationship between ε and Ze, with the information transfer from ε to Ze systematically dominating throughout the entire height range of precipitation particles. The bidirectional original ρ was far higher than the 99% quantile of the surrogate distribution, and the coupling was enhanced after removing the influence of temperature and humidity, indicating that it was independent of the thermodynamic covariant background. The causal intensity in the ice crystal growth zone above 6 km was approximately 3 times the average in the liquid zone, indicating that atmospheric turbulence is one of the key factors driving the growth of precipitation particles in freezing weather and that the effect is concentrated in the dominant layer of ice crystals. This finding provides a practical basis for improving the parameterization scheme and numerical prediction of the precipitation particle microphysical processes in freezing weather.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Hyperspectral imaging (HSI) is an important imaging modality in the field of optical remote sensing, capable of simultaneously capturing spatial structures and continuous spectral responses related to material properties. Therefore, compared with conventional red-green-blue (RGB) images, it can provide richer representations for understanding dynamic scenes. However, most existing video object segmentation methods have been developed for RGB videos, while high-quality hyperspectral video datasets with frame-by-frame pixel-level annotations remain scarce. To address this gap, this study constructed a hyperspectral video object segmentation dataset, comprising hyperspectral videos and their corresponding three-channel false-color RGB videos derived from the same HSI data, with pixel-level annotations provided for each frame. The dataset contains three spectral subsets: visible (VIS), near-infrared (NIR), and red-edge near-infrared (RedNIR). Using this dataset, we developed SSAM-SAM2, a hyperspectral video object segmentation network built on Segment Anything Model 2 (SAM 2). The network includes a spectral–spatial interaction enhancement (SSIE) module designed to identify informative spectral bands, suppress interference from redundant and noisy spectral channels, and integrate hyperspectral cues with the spatial-structure features of the corresponding false-color RGB images. In addition, the network introduces an Adaptive Correlation Memory Selection (ACMS) module that regulates memory sampling and selection according to variations in target state and inter-frame feature correlation. This design reduces the influence of redundant or unreliable historical information and improves temporal memory reliability under target deformation, motion variations, and background interference. Extensive experiments show that SSAM-SAM2 outperforms the selected comparison methods on the VIS, NIR, and RedNIR subsets under the evaluated settings. These results demonstrate the value of the proposed dataset and indicate that spectral–spatial enhancement and temporal adaptive memory mechanisms contribute to improving hyperspectral video object segmentation performance.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Small ship detection in synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery is limited by feature loss at coarse strides, sea-clutter false alarms, and the sensitivity of small boxes to annotation offsets. We propose CaSA-DETR, a detector built on RT-DETRv2 with three components. CFAR-Attention is a CFAR-inspired local contrast normalisation that reweights the stride-8 and stride-16 features bypassing the Transformer encoder, using sliding-window background statistics. Scatter-Aware Context Aggregation (SACA) collects context at four dilation rates with parallel dilated depthwise-separable convolutions and branch-adaptive gating. Inner-GIoU computes the regression loss on the inner 49% of each box, intended to reduce the influence of boundary offsets on targets smaller than 32 by 32 pixels. On a benchmark of 44,217 training images (73,407 instances, all under 32 by 32 pixels), the full model with a ResNet-101-vd backbone at 1024 by 1024 input reaches an average precision (AP) of 60.75% and an F1 score of 90.10%, 3.96 AP above D-FINE at the same input size. A cross of backbone, input size and modules shows that most of the gain over the original RT-DETRv2 baseline comes from the higher input, and that the three modules add 1.0 to 1.4 AP at every backbone and input size tested. On 2244 ship-free images, the modules cut false alarms by about 30% at a confidence of 0.5, at a cost of 0.66% more parameters and 2.3% more floating-point operations (FLOPs). The three modules improve AP at low cost.

</details>

Abstract (英文): In response to the application limitations of single-source navigation systems for their susceptibility to interference, insufficient positioning accuracy and limited scenario adaptability in complex environments, as well as the increasingly complex demands for high-precision, high-robustness, all-weather and all-scenario navigation services in fields such as the low-altitude economy, intelligent driving and specialized operations, multi-source fusion navigation technology is accelerating its evolution. This manuscript systematically reviews the development trajectory from reliance on a single navigation system to multi-source intelligent integrated navigation, dividing it into the following four evolutionary stages: single-system dominance, loose-tight coupling, deep integration and intelligent ubiquitous navigation stage. It focuses on a comparative analysis of the core characteristics, fusion modes, key technological breakthroughs, and practical limitations and bottlenecks of multi-source fusion technologies at each stage, with the aim of providing comprehensive reference for technological research and development, solution design and industrial implementation in related fields.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite ocean-color remote sensing is used to detect harmful algal blooms (HABs), yet detection capability does not necessarily ensure reliable interpretation or transferable performance. If these limitations are overlooked when developing cross-regional or global automated HAB early-warning systems, biological claims may be overstated and regional changes in false-alarm or missed-detection rates may go unnoticed, potentially compromising warning reliability and related management decisions. We develop an evidence-constrained framework to evaluate both dimensions: reliability, defined by whether the observation–processing–inference chain supports the intended biological claim, and transferability, defined by whether task-relevant performance is retained under changes in time, region, sensor, or optical environment. A structured Web of Science search yielded 1212 unique records, from which 28 purposively prioritized full-text core studies underwent coding for validation design, cross-domain evidence, error, and uncertainty. The synthesis identifies an evidentiary gap between optical detection capability and biological claim specificity, while fixed-configuration cross-domain evidence was scarce and performance retention remained unquantified. Satellite observations can support bloom presence, optical phenotypes, and selected taxon-targeted applications, but specific claims require specific independent biological references. Reliability is constrained by reference and labeling practices, preprocessing, observation availability, and incomplete uncertainty characterization, while evidence for transferability is limited by insufficient source–target evaluation. We propose a framework linking claim–evidence alignment, independent validation, domain-shift assessment, and uncertainty reporting. Reliable and transferable HAB remote sensing should move beyond detection outputs toward evidence-supported, uncertainty-aware products for monitoring, early warning, and decision-making.

</details>
