Abstract (英文): Building-footprint errors can propagate into geospatial assets generated from remote-sensing imagery. This study evaluated Mask2Former–FGMBRM, which applies feature-guided residual refinement to Mask2Former query-mask predictions and integrates extracted footprints with documented terrain data and geospatial processing for footprint-based urban asset generation. Six models were evaluated on 518 WHU held-out images using common final references and metric definitions while retaining model-specific pipelines. The tune-selected Mask2Former baseline and FGMBRM achieved AP values of 72.03% and 73.49%, respectively, a 1.47-percentage-point improvement. FGMBRM also achieved 89.01% binary IoU and 91.86% object F1, leading these measures and AP among the six configurations, whereas SegFormer had the highest boundary F1. Controlled ablations showed metric-dependent trade-offs, with simpler variants leading selected outcomes and no consistent advantage from query-conditioned guidance. The original INRIA comparison remained limited. In a separate query-capacity diagnostic, AP at common maxDets = 100 increased from 20.66% to 23.96%, while retaining more outputs increased recall and false positives. The results support footprint-based urban asset generation and visualization, with remaining dense-scene and geometric limitations.

Abstract (英文): Adverse weather degrades visibility, contrast, edges, and local target details in aerial imagery, while small object size, viewing geometry, and onboard constraints further amplify detection failures. This review examines how weather-induced degradation propagates from image formation to feature damage and detection errors, and evaluates the evidence supporting existing robustness claims. A structured search of studies published between 2015 and 2026 identified 174 candidate records, which were analysed in terms of degradation mechanisms, methodological pathways, datasets, evaluation protocols, training dependencies, and deployment conditions. Existing methods are grouped into input restoration, joint restoration–detection learning, degradation-aware representation, domain adaptation and generalisation, multimodal and temporal compensation, and lightweight deployment. The evidence shows that improved visual quality does not consistently translate into better detection performance, while many reported gains depend on paired data, target-domain access, external priors, additional sensors, or computational overhead. Current evidence remains strongly concentrated on haze, with limited validation for natural rain, snow, dust, compound weather, unseen-weather generalisation, uncertainty, and closed-loop flight. Future research should therefore prioritise realistic weather data, controlled evaluation, risk-aware assessment, and onboard validation.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monitoring climate change is crucial to society, industry, and the environment to assess their vulnerability to such extreme temperature events and plan adaptation and mitigation strategies. Temperature-based climate indices, which are used for climate monitoring, are usually calculated using near-surface air temperature (NSAT) from weather stations. A limitation of NSAT is the inhomogeneity of station distribution across the globe, with some regions under-sampled. This study evaluates the potential of using land surface temperature (LST) retrieved via satellite to calculate temperature indices, because low Earth-orbiting satellites can observe almost all of the Earth. This work compares indices calculated from NSAT to those produced from LST, and the indices are aggregated by Intergovernmental Panel on Climate Change AR6 regions. Results show that there is often moderate to high correlation between indices produced using NSAT and LST. In addition, it is found that monthly to seasonal changes in indices are similar for both variables, particularly in percentile-based indices. Threshold-based indices could not be calculated for some regions due to low data availability, and therefore this work also applies “relative” indices, where indices are normalised relative to the amount of data available. These generally also demonstrate high correlations between NSAT and LST and can better show when events, such as heatwaves, occur. Lastly, this work presents the potential of an LST-based temperature indices dataset and how uncertainties could be evaluated.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Inland lake ecosystems function as active nodes in the global carbon cycle, yet reliable basin-scale estimates of particulate organic carbon concentration (CPOC) remain scarce owing to the optical complexity of these environments. In this study, POC denotes the total particulate organic carbon pool, including organic matter from living and non-living particles. Here we present a semi-analytical retrieval scheme tailored to Sentinel-2 Multispectral Instrument (MSI) data and applied to 90 large eutrophic lakes (each >10 km2) within the Yangtze–Huaihe Plain (YHP) of eastern China. The scheme reconstructs the particulate absorption coefficient at 665 nm, ap(665), from reflectance measurements at 665, 705, and 740 nm, reducing the interference of colored dissolved organic matter (aCDOM) absorption and particulate backscattering (bb). When evaluated against 60 field observations in the predefined concentration-stratified holdout, the algorithm achieved MAE = 0.59 mg/L, RMSE = 0.73 mg/L, and MAPE = 46.89%—outperforming three widely cited benchmark algorithms—and showed preliminary transfer potential in the two estuarine datasets. MSI-derived CPOC maps from 2018 to 2024 revealed systematic east-to-west and center-to-periphery concentration gradients across the 90-lake assemblage, shaped by catchment nutrient delivery, basin hydrodynamics, and phytoplankton standing stock. Correlation and attribution analyses identified chlorophyll-a concentration (CChla), atmospheric pressure, and total phosphorus (CTP) as the three dominant environmental modulators of CPOC variability. The framework provides a field-calibrated approach for examining regional lake CPOC patterns. Its satellite application and ability to distinguish small concentration differences require further independent validation and uncertainty assessment.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Biomass burning smoke (BBS) emitted from Southeast Asia can be transported through regional atmospheric circulation, exerting significant impacts on air quality and climate over southwestern China. However, continuous observations of the vertical structures and optical properties of transported BBS remain limited. This study used one year of continuous multi-wavelength polarization Raman lidar observations at Mengzi (23.37°N, 103.38°E) in Yunnan, China, to identify transboundary BBS transport events and characterize their optical properties. Eight transboundary BBS events originating from Southeast Asia were identified using a comprehensive data processing framework involving aerosol extinction coefficient, backscatter coefficient, lidar ratio, particle linear depolarization ratio (PDR), and Ångström exponent. The transported smoke layers were mainly distributed below 4 km, exhibiting significantly enhanced aerosol extinction coefficients of 0.22–0.32 km−1 (compared with 0.02–0.06 km−1 for background aerosols). The 532 nm lidar ratios of the smoke layers (49.0–68.3 sr) were higher than those of the background aerosols (31.6–39.0 sr). The particle linear depolarization ratios ranged from 0.050 to 0.097 at 532 nm and from 0.14 to 0.19 at 355 nm, revealing a pronounced wavelength dependence. These values indicate measurable particle depolarization but do not independently establish particle morphology or the degree of smoke aging. Hybrid Single-Particle Lagrangian Integrated Trajectory (HYSPLIT) backward trajectory analysis together with fire hotspot observations revealed that this smoke mainly originated from biomass burning regions over the Indochina Peninsula and was transported to Yunnan across northern Myanmar, Laos, and northern Vietnam. This study provides important observational evidence of transport pathways, vertical distribution, and optical properties of transboundary BBS aerosols transported from Southeast Asia to southwest China.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Daily water-level changes in alpine closed lakes integrate lake-surface exchange, cryosphere processes, basin storage, and runoff routing, yet fixed calendar seasons and synchronous predictors may mix contrasting hydrological states and misalign antecedent forcing. This study developed a Hydro-Lag framework for Qinghai Lake, China’s largest inland lake, during 2014–2023 to improve daily reconstruction and driver attribution through dynamic hydrological process periods and time-lag effects. Satellite altimetry, optical and thermal imagery, hydrometeorological reanalysis, lake ice, and runoff data were integrated with water-balance constraints. The frozen (T1) and ablation (T2) periods were identified from lake ice transitions and a positive degree-day (PDD) threshold estimated from ice thickness. Meanwhile, basin wetness, represented by the antecedent precipitation index (API), distinguished the wet (T3) and dry (T4) periods. Candidate lags from cross-correlation analysis were refined through repeated cross-validation and used in an ensemble of extreme gradient boosting (XGBoost) and ExtraTrees; cross-fitted predictions were interpreted with SHapley Additive exPlanations (SHAP). Alignments differed markedly: selected core variables in T1 aligned mainly at 10–12 days; T2 used 1–2 days runoff, soil-moisture, and evapotranspiration offsets together with a same-day 14-day M_snow accumulation; T3 runoff and soil moisture at 3–5 days and precipitation-related variables at 8–9 days; and T4 antecedent basin variables at 11–24 days. Against an independently tuned SeasonRAW baseline, Hydro-Lag increased test R2 from 0.551 to 0.793 and reduced RMSE from 14.26 to 8.51 mm d−1. OOF SHAP indicated period-dependent patterns in modeled predictor contributions: mixed contributions in T1, wetness–storage emphasis in T2–T3, and antecedent storage and thermo-evaporative contributions in T4. Conditional responses showed thresholds and sign reversals across thermal, wetness, and event-magnitude gradients. The framework provides a process-aware basis for assessing daily lake–basin interactions in data-sparse alpine closed basins.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Fuel moisture codes are numerical indices used to estimate the moisture content of different layers of forest fuels and are widely applied to assess the ignition potential and behavior of wildfires. Terrain-corrected L-band ALOS-2 PALSAR-2 synthetic aperture radar (SAR) imagery predicted the Drought Code (DC) and Duff Moisture Code (DMC) of the Canadian Forest Fire Danger Rating System in mountainous forests of British Columbia. Random forest regression models were developed using backscatter, decomposition parameters, and polarimetric discriminators obtained from forest (high-biomass, conifer-dominated), sparse (medium-biomass, open canopy), and unforested (low-biomass, herbaceous-dominated) fuel types near weather stations. Out-of-bag predicted values were compared against weather station-derived values. Models trained using SAR data predicted the DC with moderate accuracy in sparse (R2 = 0.49, RMSE = 165.65) and forest sites (R2 = 0.39, RMSE = 173.80), and with low accuracy in unforested sites (R2 = 0.15, RMSE = 205.96). Models trained using SAR data predicted the DMC with moderate accuracy in sparse (R2 = 0.64, RMSE = 26.07) and unforested sites (R2 = 0.47, RMSE = 31.52), and with low accuracy in forest sites (R2 = 0.12, RMSE = 40.71). Incorporating SAR data and day of year (DOY) improved the accuracy of DC models. Errors in both DC and DMC increased when models were evaluated with SAR data from weather stations not used in training, suggesting limited spatial transferability beyond training weather stations. Results suggest that terrain-corrected L-band polarimetric SAR has potential for predicting fuel moisture in mountainous and forested environments. Differences in surface and soil conditions across the fire environment must be accounted for to improve the spatial transferability of the models.

Abstract (英文): Data assimilation generally assumes that observation-minus-background (OMB) departures follow an unbiased Gaussian distribution. Therefore, bias correction (BC) is essential for the effective assimilation of satellite radiances. This study applies four machine learning (ML) approaches to reduce systematic biases in clear-sky observations from the water-vapor-sensitive channels 9 and 10 of the Fengyun-4A (FY-4A) Advanced Geosynchronous Radiation Imager (AGRI). These methods include random forest (RF), extreme gradient boosting (XGBoost), multilayer perceptron (MLP), and the Kolmogorov–Arnold network (KAN). The machine-learning-based bias correction (MLBC) methods are evaluated for Typhoon In-Fa (2021) using the Weather Research and Forecasting Data Assimilation (WRFDA) system. Experiments without BC and with conventional variational bias correction (VarBC) are designed for comparison. Compared with VARBC, the four MLBC methods better capture nonlinear OMB biases and improve the statistical distributions of the departures. They produce more symmetric OMB distributions for channel 9 and distributions closer to the Gaussian distribution for channel 10, with smaller OMB STDVs and RMSEs than VARBC over both land and ocean for channel 10. After assimilation, KANBC produces the smallest OMA STDV and RMSE for both channels. For the Typhoon In-Fa case examined here, KANBC yields the smallest track errors among the experiments. In particular, KAN-based BC reduces the anomalously strong positive 500 hPa geopotential height increments over the continental high pressure and western North Pacific subtropical high pressure. The resulting initial conditions improve the environmental steering flow and reduce the northwestward track bias. Channel sensitivity experiments were further conducted for water-vapor-sensitive channels 9 and 10 of the FY-4A AGRI using the KAN-based BC method. The results show that single-channel assimilation performs better than dual-channel assimilation. Assimilating channel 10 alone produces the smallest track errors and lower analysis RMSEs relative to ERA5 than assimilating both channels.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Standing dead trees (SDTs) play a critical role in forest biodiversity assessment, disturbance monitoring, and carbon cycling. High-resolution optical satellite imagery offers an opportunity for large-scale SDT detection by providing finer spatial detail than moderate-resolution sensors. Despite this advantage, isolated SDTs may occupy only part of a PlanetScope pixel, allowing their signals to mix with those of live canopy, non-woody vegetation, and shadows. As a result, it is necessary to quantify signal detectability and detection stability across these conditions. To address this issue, this study uses PlanetScope imagery at 3 m resolution and Uncrewed Aerial Vehicle (UAV)-derived reference annotations to develop a multi-level, response-based SDT framework for analyzing SDT detectability from the aspect of the segmentation models, SDT density, and SDT patch sizes. UAV annotations are first aggregated to the PlanetScope pixel scale to generate hard labels and soft labels. The deep and machine learning models are then trained to select a stable reference semantic model and assess the consistency of SDT detection responses across architectures. All deep learning segmentation models produced consistent detectability responses, with HRNet achieving the best performance, with a binary F1 of 0.86. A single-pixel SDT signal became evident mainly when SDT cover proportion reached the 70 %-80 %, whereas neighborhood support lowered this change to a local SDT density of 20 %–30 %. Patch-level analysis indicated more stable detection for SDT patches larger than 90–180 m 2 . Furthermore, reliable PlanetScope detection is associated with SDT dominance, spatial aggregation, and high local SDT density . The proposed framework provides a detectability analysis approach for evaluating the stability of SDT detection in 3 m satellite imagery and the consistency of these responses across semantic segmentation models, linking high-resolution satellite imagery probability mapping with ultra-high-resolution UAV-based observations.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Polar clouds play important roles in regulating the surface energy budget, atmospheric moisture cycle, precipitation, and sea-ice variability, while their occurrence, vertical structure, thermodynamic phase, and microphysical properties remain difficult to characterize consistently across the Arctic and Antarctic. Satellite remote sensing enables broad-scale monitoring of polar clouds, yet high-albedo snow and ice surfaces, weak cloud–surface thermal contrast, frequent temperature inversions, and persistent mixed-phase conditions make cloud detection and property retrieval substantially more difficult than at lower latitudes. This review synthesizes progress during the quarter-century 2001–2025 in polar cloud satellite remote sensing with a focus on satellite-based polar cloud detection, cloud phase identification, and microphysical retrieval. It first analyzes the synergistic monitoring capabilities of multi-source satellite sensors, including passive multispectral imagers, active radars and lidars, and microwave radiometers. It then explores in depth the evolution of cloud detection methods from static physical thresholds to deep learning algorithms that integrate spatial information and multimodal features. For cloud phase identification and microphysical retrieval, this paper reviews the physical principles and technical limitations of representative methods, including threshold and Lookup Table (LUT) methods, Optimal Estimation Method (OEM), variational and Bayesian retrievals, analytical and empirical methods, and machine-learning approaches, with particular attention to the challenges associated with ice–liquid phase partitioning and vertical heterogeneity in polar mixed-phase clouds. Finally, it identifies the deficiencies in current research regarding the application of far-infrared and high-frequency submillimeter-wave bands, the construction of full-spatiotemporal coverage observation networks, and the physical consistency of data-driven models, and outlines priorities for developing a continuous, multi-dimensional polar cloud monitoring system based on coordinated high- and low-orbit observations, recent and forthcoming satellite instruments, and physically constrained artificial intelligence.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate cropland change detection from high-resolution remote sensing images is essential for agricultural resource monitoring, land management, and food security. Existing methods struggle with two primary challenges: (1) severe pseudo-change noise and false alarms, caused by sole reliance on simple spatial or temporal difference enhancement, and (2) inadequate fine-grained cropland change detection, resulting from boundary detail degradation during fixed interpolation decoding. To address these issues, we propose a S patio T emporal D ifference- E nhancement N etwork (STDENet) with semantic flow alignment for effective cropland change detection from high-resolution remote sensing images. Built on a pyramid vision transformer V2 (PVT-V2) encoder, STDENet integrates three key innovations: a spatiotemporal dual-branch difference enhancement (STDDE) module that jointly captures temporal variations and spatial discrepancies to suppress irrelevant responses from unchanged or spectrally similar regions; an adaptive boundary-aware (ABA) module that dynamically extracts four-directional texture cues to enhance boundary representations; and a semantic flow-aligned decoding (SFAD) module that predicts semantic flow fields to warp high-level difference features and align them with low-level spatial details for fine-grained structure recovery. We evaluate the STDENet on one self-constructed dataset, DYCD, and two public benchmark datasets, CLCD and Hi-CNA. Comparative experiments confirm that STDENet consistently outperforms existing state-of-the-art methods on all three datasets, improving IoU by 3.41 %, 4.90 %, and 1.98 % over the second-best methods on DYCD, CLCD, and Hi-CNA, respectively. By enhancing global structural information through the spatial branch and local change responses through the temporal branch, the spatiotemporal interaction design improves the feature separability of cropland changes. Semantic flow alignment helps restore fine-grained details by producing a more stable decoding process for local contours in cropland change patches. STDENet also demonstrates stronger resistance to interference and better adaptability to phenological variations among crop growth, pre-sowing, and post-harvest periods. Large-scale applications further indicate its generalization ability across heterogeneous cropland structures.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The low spatial resolution (300–400 km) of Gravity Recovery and Climate Experience (GRACE) and the 11-month data gap between GRACE and GRACE Follow-On (GRACE-FO) complicate the evaluation of long-term ice mass loss at sub-region scale. Although several studies have downscaled GRACE data for water storage evaluation, efforts to downscale ice mass change are limited. Here, we utilized multiscale geographically-weighted regression (MGWR) to improve the spatial resolution of GRACE-derived ice mass change from 0.25° to 5 km in Greenland Ice Sheet (GrIS). The reconstructed results after downscaling demonstrate consistent temporal variation and reduced noise compared to other reconstruction methods for sub-regions and the entire GrIS. They can capture finer signals of ice mass change while maintaining original spatial characteristics. Validation against free air gravity anomalies changes also demonstrates the superior performance of the downscaled results over the reconstructed CSR Mascon data. For overlapping lines, the mean correlation coefficient (CC) increases from 0.13 to 0.32, with the mean root mean square error (RMSE) decreasing from 1.36 to 1.13. At the intersection points, the mean CC improves from 0.06 to 0.30, and the mean RMSE is reduced from 1.33 to 1.05. They are further used to detect the contributions of Greenland Blocking (GB) and North Atlantic Oscillation (NAO) to high-resolution ice mass change through precipitation and runoff in four sub-periods, during which the driving factors affecting ice mass change differ significantly. Additionally, NAO and GB can influence ice mass change through solid ice discharge by impacting surface temperature and sea-ice concentration.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vegetation Optical Depth (VOD) is a key microwave-derived remote sensing parameter reflecting vegetation water content and supporting ecological and carbon-cycle studies. However, existing VOD products have coarse spatial resolution (∼9–36 km), limiting fine-scale applications. Statistical and machine-learning downscaling methods remain immature, lacking reliability and physical consistency, especially over large heterogeneous regions. To address this research gap, this study proposes the Physics-informed Differencing Downscaling Framework (PhyDiF). PhyDiF integrates a differencing paradigm with radiative transfer model (RTM)-based physical constraints. Rather than directly predicting absolute VOD values, the differencing paradigm predicts the differences in VOD ( Δ V O D ) induced by spatial resolution differences. The predicted Δ V O D is then combined with the coarse-resolution baseline VOD to reconstruct high-resolution absolute VOD. In addition, an RTM-based physical constraint is incorporated into a joint loss function to improve VOD prediction accuracy while promoting consistency between the predicted VOD and the corresponding brightness temperature (TB) simulated using the RTM. At the 9 km scale, direct validation of the differencing paradigm within PhyDiF and model selection among various baseline models show that U‑Net performs best in predicting Δ V O D , achieving a correlation coefficient with the true values of 0.854, with RMSE and MAE of only 0.016 and 0.011, respectively. Based on this, PhyDiF was constructed by incorporating physical constraints, generating a 3‑day 1 km PhyDiF VOD dataset over China from 2015 to 2021. Comprehensive evaluation shows that the PhyDiF VOD product outperforms the baseline product (MT‑DCA) and traditional interpolation results (Cubic) in spatial details, clearly depicting texture information and VOD spatial heterogeneity that more closely resemble actual land surface features. Correlations with above‑ground biomass (AGB), canopy height (CH), and leaf area index (LAI) are significantly improved compared with existing methods, both across China and in typical local areas. For example, in the low‑VOD area, compared with the Cubic method, the differencing‑paradigm‑based U‑Net and PhyDiF achieve improvements in correlation with AGB, CH, and LAI of 19.13 %/5.83 %/9.48 % and 32.17 %/10.42 %/17.43 %, respectively, while PhyDiF, incorporating physical constraints, outperforms U‑Net by 10.95 %, 4.33 %, and 7.26 %, respectively. In temporal validation, the product accurately exhibited the seasonal cycles and interannual dynamic trends of vegetation phenology, maintaining high consistency with MT‑DCA. Further scale consistency testing confirmed a correlation of 0.9997 with MT‑DCA and a standard deviation of only 0.007. Overall, PhyDiF provides a generalizable and reliable framework for generating high‑resolution VOD, advancing vegetation microwave remote sensing technology.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Monitoring nutrient dynamics is essential for assessing eutrophication in coastal waters, yet conventional in situ observations provide limited spatial coverage. This study evaluated the capability of Sentinel-3 Ocean and Land Colour Instrument (OLCI)-derived nutrient products to represent seasonal and interannual dynamics of total nitrogen (TN) and total phosphorus (TP) in Pärnu Bay, Gulf of Riga, during 2016–2024. Nutrient concentrations were estimated using regionally adapted OLCI retrieval algorithms and compared with in situ observations at pixel, seasonal, and annual scales. An additional comparison with operational Copernicus Marine Environment Monitoring Service (CMEMS) nutrient modelling products was carried out. Pixel-level validation yielded moderate agreement between OLCI-derived and in situ observations (TN: R 2 = 0.50, NRMSE = 26.5%; TP: R 2 = 0.33, NRMSE = 30.9%). Independent temporal validation using 2022–2024 observations retained comparable agreement (TN: R 2 = 0.44 and TP: R 2 = 0.47). However, spatial and temporal aggregation resulted in stronger overall correspondence and substantially lower error metrics. Seasonal TN products achieved an R 2 of 0.75 and NRMSE of 14.7%, while annual products reached an R 2 of 0.68 and NRMSE of 8.6%. Similar improvements were observed for TP, where annual aggregation increased R 2 to 0.54 and reduced NRMSE to 10.0%. The OLCI-derived products captured seasonal nutrient variability, interannual nutrient tendencies, and spatial nutrient gradients associated with riverine influence. These findings demonstrate that moderate pixel-level retrieval accuracy does not necessarily limit bay-scale monitoring capability. Spatially and temporally aggregated Sentinel-3 OLCI products provide a valuable complement to conventional monitoring programmes and show considerable potential for long-term eutrophication assessment in optically complex Baltic coastal waters.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Understanding the spatial and temporal variability of global surface warming is important for anticipating regional climate extremes and potential tipping points. However, conventional temperature indicators based on spatial averaging may obscure the evolution of persistent warming hotspots and the regional processes associated with surface temperature changes. Here, we analyze satellite-derived global land–ocean surface temperature (GLST) data from 2003 to 2023 and propose a three-dimensional (latitude-longitude-time) spatiotemporal clustering framework to characterize global warming patterns and identify persistent heat clusters. Globally, GLST increased at a rate of 0.025 ± 0.007 K/y, with land warming occurring 60% faster (0.034 ± 0.012 K/y) than over oceans (0.021 ± 0.006 K/y). Warming peaked prominently at ∼ 45°N (0.075 K/y in the Western Pacific; 0.066 K/y over Europe), primarily driven by dynamics in snow-climate zones and boreal forests. Spatiotemporal clustering revealed expanding heat anomalies in northern high latitudes (>45°N), where intensity (+0.027 K/y) and spatial coverage (+0.322 × 10⁶ km 2 /y) were positive; both metrics were larger in 2013–2023 than in 2003–2012, although the short-term trend is sensitive to individual extreme years. Notably, over land, non-radiative factors, including latent heat flux and soil moisture, were associated with regional differences in surface warming, with evaporative cooling effects reducing warming in several moisture-limited regions. These findings highlight the value of satellite observations and spatiotemporal clustering for identifying global warming hotspots and developing targeted adaptation strategies.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Forests in the southeastern United States (US) are frequently disturbed by wind events, such as hurricanes, tornadoes, and severe windstorms, affecting ecosystem resilience, biodiversity, and carbon storage. Monitoring post-disturbance forest changes is challenging due to the limited availability of spatially continuous and temporally high-resolution canopy height data. To address these limitations, we evaluated the potential of PlanetScope data to assess forest structure and thematic changes across three wind-affected sites in the southeastern US: Oakmulgee (tornado), Flagg Mountain (straight-line wind), and Weeks Bay (hurricane). We used multi-stereo PlanetScope imagery to generate photogrammetric point clouds, which were processed to extract a suite of discrete canopy height metrics at different spatial resolutions (3 m, 10 m, 15 m, and 20 m) and validated using airborne lidar-derived canopy height metrics. Random Forest (RF) regression models were then developed using PlanetScope imagery, acquired concurrently with airborne lidar across study sites, and the trained models were applied to produce pre- and post-disturbance canopy height maps. Similarly, RF classification models were developed for three scenarios (airborne lidar acquisition date, pre-wind, and post-wind), and applied to generate forest/non-forest maps for pre- and post-disturbance periods, enabling assessment of thematic changes following wind disturbance. Results indicate that upper canopy height percentiles (95th and 98th) aggregated at a 20 m grid resolution produced the strongest agreement with airborne lidar measurements (correlation coefficient (r) = 0.32–0.33, Root Mean Squared Error (RMSE) = 7.57–9.05 m). When the comparison was limited to grid cells where PlanetScope and lidar heights differed by 7 m or less, agreement improved (r = 0.68–0.79, RMSE = 3.70–4.35 m). RF canopy height estimation models showed moderate accuracy across study sites (R 2 = 0.51–0.54, RMSE = 2.45–4.31 m). Multi-temporal analysis using generated 20 m canopy height maps revealed substantial canopy height reductions after wind disturbance, affecting 40.47% of Oakmulgee site, 36.52% of Flagg Mountain, and 60.92% of Weeks Bay. Overall, the approach provides a promising framework for assessing and monitoring forest disturbance impacts. It captures post-disturbance changes in forest structure with an ability to provide spatially explicit and timely information on canopy structure and thematic dynamics, particularly in areas where airborne lidar or field measurements are limited.

</details>

Abstract (英文): How the relative importance of environmental factors shifts during post-fire vegetation recovery remains underexplored in temperate forests with complex terrain and mixed-species canopies. This study examined the 2022 Uljin–Samcheok fire in South Korea (16,302 ha in total), focusing on the Uljin portion and integrating annual dNBR (differenced normalized burn ratio) multi-temporal data from Landsat 8/9 imagery for 2022–2025 with meteorological, topographic, and forest-structure variables at a 500 m grid scale, using the 892 grid cells in which dNBR ≥ 0.1 pixels covered at least 10% of the cell area. Annual ordinary least squares (OLS) models with a consistent structure enabled direct comparison of regression coefficients over time, providing a framework for evaluating temporal shifts in recovery correlates. Post-fire recovery dynamics were characterized not by a simple transition from terrain-dominated to meteorology-dominated controls, but by driver-specific temporal shifts. The positive association of slope with dNBR intensified through 2024 and then attenuated sharply in 2025, its standardized coefficient falling to roughly one-quarter of the 2024 peak (0.449 → 0.116), while northness maintained a persistent negative association throughout the recovery period. In contrast to Mediterranean studies, where topographic associations strengthen over time, this sharp attenuation of the slope constraint in the fourth year may be consistent with a recovery pathway in which temperate climatic conditions and the resprouting capacity of broadleaf forests weaken the persistence of topographic constraints. Antecedent seasonal meteorological associations diminished over time—from four significant variables in 2022 and seven in 2023 to three in 2024—and none remained significant in 2025, indicating that within the burned area the explanatory role of antecedent weather diminished as recovery progressed. This framework captures driver-specific shifts rarely addressed in existing studies and offers a reproducible approach applicable to other temperate forest fire sites.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Artificial light at night (ALAN) distribution is crucial for a variety of studies, e.g., assessing spatially heterogeneous light pollution and resilient environments. Thus, new trends require mapping ALAN from a 2D plane toward complex 3D space. However, a robust method to estimate high-resolution ALAN distribution on 3D urban surfaces (including rooftop, façade, and ground) is still lacking. To tackle this challenge, we developed a super-resolution augmented spatiotemporal modelling framework with three hierarchical modules. First, SDGSAT-1 panchromatic and RGB imagery were fused to enhance multispectral ALAN information from 40-m to 10-m. Second, irradiance was redistributed to 1-m 3D point clouds using land use/land cover (LULC), building morphology, and an empirically supported vertical profile. Third, the estimates were evaluated using independent proxy observations from QMX-1 imagery and nighttime street-view imagery. Compared with a baseline 3D ALAN product generated directly from raw 10-m panchromatic irradiance, the proposed method increased the mean Spearman correlation coefficient from 0.498 to 0.625 across the four LULC types and three RGB bands, corresponding to a relative improvement of approximately 25.4%. The clearest gains occurred in commercial and industrial areas, whereas educational and residential areas showed weaker or non-significant improvements. The framework provides a scalable spatial redistribution approach for city-scale 3D ALAN representation, while remaining subject to uncertainties from simplified radiative assumptions, parameter transfer, and proxy validation data.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate monitoring of Spartina alterniflora eradication is essential for evaluating coastal wetland restoration and supporting invasive-species management. However, vegetation removal and natural surface variation can produce similar spectral changes in heterogeneous tidal flats, complicating image-based detection. To address this issue, we propose COAST ( Coastal Online Adaptive Spatiotemporal Transformer ), an online ecological-prior-guided bitemporal Transformer for mapping potential S. alterniflora eradication areas in the Jiangsu coastal tidal flats, China. The framework uses geographically weighted discriminant analysis (GWDA) posterior probabilities to represent spatially heterogeneous relationships between S. alterniflora pre-eradication spectral features and removal-related change. These probabilities provide soft supervision for a lightweight online prior encoder, whose output modulates bitemporal features through spatial prior gating. Training uses spatial-block out-of-fold GWDA predictions, and inference uses paired images with the prior generated from the pre-eradication image. Under a spatially separated evaluation design, COAST achieved the highest mean F1-score (95.00 %) and IoU (90.48 %) among the evaluated baselines across three random-seed runs. It also achieved a boundary F1-score of 81.25 %, a 95th-percentile Hausdorff distance (HD95) of 256.04 m, and a changed-area fraction mean absolute error of 0.82 percentage points. Ablation experiments supported the contribution of prior-guided learning relative to the BiT backbone. Evaluation on manually annotated cross-year patches yielded F1-scores of 88.27 % for 2020–2023 and 93.13 % for 2020–2025, supporting temporal transfer within the sampled coastal settings. Local errors included agricultural-background false positives and incomplete target recovery. COAST provides an image-based workflow for repeated regional screening of eradication-related change and the prioritisation of follow-up verification.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Dothistroma needle blight (DNB) is a major foliar disease of Pinus radiata , an important plantation species in several Southern Hemisphere countries. DNB management and resistance breeding depend on subjective, labour-intensive visual severity scoring that is difficult to scale. Prior UAV studies have used broad severity categories and crown-mean reflectance, discarding both the ordinal structure of the field grade and the within-crown spatial pattern of infection. This study evaluated whether UAV multispectral imagery can quantify tree-scale DNB severity while preserving the ordinal 5 % field grading, treating adjacent-class errors as less severe than distant ones. From imagery of two trials in the North Island, New Zealand, 8,136 manually delineated crowns were characterised by 552 candidate features (82 vegetation indices and 10 raw bands, each summarised by six within-crown distributional statistics). After collinearity filtering, 40 features were used to fit Ordinal Forest (OF) and Frequency-Adjusted Borders Ordinal Forest (fabOF) models at three granularities (5, 10, and 19 severity classes). At 19-class resolution, predictions fell within ± 10 points of the true severity for approximately 80 % of crowns. Quadratic weighted kappa rose with granularity, consistent with finer models erring predominantly by small, adjacent-class amounts. fabOF improved ordinal ranking, with markedly less underestimation between 50 and 70 % severity, whereas OF was sharper in the well-sampled moderate range, a calibration-balance trade-off across applications. Within-crown variability and tail-percentile statistics, not crown-mean reflectance, carried the strongest signal (SHAP analysis), consistent with DNB’s patchy progression. UAV multispectral imagery can therefore support fine-grained DNB severity assessment, an automated complement to visual scoring for management and resistance phenotyping, demonstrated here across two juvenile trials.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A thorough understanding of the global carbon pools and cycle is essential for understanding and predicting the effects of climate change. Coastal waters play a key role in the global carbon cycle but remain poorly understood due to their optical complexity, high spatial variability, and sensitivity to climate change. Satellite remote sensing data can provide high spatial and temporal resolution for carbon monitoring at local, regional, and global scales. This study estimates the potential of Sentinel-2 MSI (S2) and Sentinel-3 OLCI (S3) for mapping carbon fractions in coastal waters. In situ hyperspectral reflectance measurements and concentrations of different carbon fractions, namely Total Organic Carbon (TOC), Dissolved Organic Carbon (DOC), Particulate Organic Carbon (POC), and Dissolved Inorganic Carbon (DIC), were used. Bio-optical and physical water parameters, as well as carbon fractions, were measured in the coastal areas of the eastern Baltic Sea from 2023 to 2024. In situ reflectance spectra were recalculated to S2 and S3 band reflectance using their respective spectral response functions. Regression models, including linear regression and machine learning methods, were developed and validated against in situ data to estimate carbon fractions. For S2, the combinations of green and red or red-edge bands achieved the best performance in carbon retrieval, with R 2 values of 0.67, 0.64, and 0.56 for TOC, DOC, and DIC, respectively. The corresponding MAPE values ranged from 14% to 18%, and biases were close to zero. Slightly higher accuracies were obtained with S3 simulations. The S3 models that used reflectance at 490, 620, and 665 nm produced the most accurate estimates for both TOC ( R 2 = 0.71) and DOC ( R 2 = 0.67). For DIC, the best predictive model combined information from 510, 665, and 709 nm bands, achieving R 2 of 0.62. Among the tested optical proxies, CDOM absorption at 443 nm showed the most consistent relationship with TOC, DOC, and DIC ( R 2 = 0.56–0.30). The study represents a significant step forward in the remote sensing of coastal waters, and if adopted, the proposed carbon-fraction products could enable substantial progress across various fields, from carbon-cycle research to monitoring and policymaking.

</details>

Abstract (英文): By the end of this decade, several space agencies and commercial operators will develop and launch the next generation of space-based Earth observation satellites. This paper focuses on imaging spectroradiometers, which measure solar radiation reflected by the Earth’s surface across the ultra-violet, visible and short-wave infrared spectral ranges using hyperspectral sensors. To enable interoperable use of datasets from this growing constellation of sensors, it is essential to understand and manage differences between the measurements of the same target under similar conditions. These differences arise from multiple factors: real variations due to instrument design (e.g., differing spectral responses or viewing geometries), differences in Level 1 processing approaches based on pre-flight and post-launch calibration strategies, differences in higher-level processing into biophysical quantities, and any residual biases between sensors caused by measurement or processing errors — some of which may not be fully captured in reported uncertainties. A consistent approach to the analysis and documentation of uncertainties supports intercomparison between datasets. By assessing whether measurements agree within the expected uncertainty associated with the comparison, or identifying and quantifying systematic deviations when they do not, it becomes possible to validate interoperability and diagnose discrepancies. This paper outlines key concepts for harmonising uncertainty analysis and reporting techniques across missions, including the use of structured uncertainty propagation frameworks such as uncertainty trees. The goal is to initiate a roadmap for understanding and quantifying uncertainties in a way that facilitates robust intercomparison of multiple space-based imaging spectroradiometer measurements.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate quantification of terrestrial gross primary production (GPP) is fundamental to understanding plant productivity, ecosystem function, and carbon-climate feedbacks. Existing approaches typically rely on meteorological, soil, and other ancillary inputs, which introduce substantial uncertainties at regional to global scales. NASA's Plankton, Aerosol, Cloud, ocean Ecosystem (PACE) mission, carrying the Ocean Color Instrument (OCI), provides the first contiguous global hyperspectral observations from space. Using 122 spectral bands from PACE, we developed a data-driven framework based on partial least squares regression to generate an 8-day global GPP product. The model achieves a robust performance (R 2 = 0.71, RMSE = 1.93 gC m −2 d −1 ), comparable to models that incorporate meteorological drivers, and outperforms approaches based on broadband reflectance or vegetation indices. PACE-derived GPP showed broad spatial correspondence with solar-induced fluorescence (SIF), including hotspots across high productivity regions such as the U.S. Corn Belt, the Amazon and Congo basins, and the Indo-Gangetic and North China Plains. This strong spatial coherence supports the physiological relevance of the PACE retrievals. Our results demonstrate that hyperspectral observations provide a direct, observation-driven framework for estimating plant productivity at the global scale, opening new opportunities for monitoring vegetation responses to environmental change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite-derived observations of inland waters are widely used in environmental monitoring and climate research, often forming the basis of long-term analyses in the absence of continuous in situ measurements. However, the influence of transient atmospheric phenomena, such as volcanic plumes, on these datasets remains insufficiently quantified. In this study, we investigate the extent to which volcanic plumes influence lake surface water temperature (LSWT), turbidity, and chlorophyll-a measurements derived from ESA Climate Change Initiative (CCI) Lakes. Using a multi-sensor approach, we leverage MISR and MODIS observations to analyse plume–lake interactions during the 2011–2012 eruption of Puyehue-Cordón Caulle (PCC), Chile. Results indicate that while standard cloud screening procedures remove a substantial proportion of plume-affected observations, residual impacts remain. Notably, lake observations are influenced not only when plumes directly overlay the lake surface but also when plumes are proximal but not visually detectable over the lake, indicating the influence of diffuse aerosol particles. Analysis of aerosol microphysical properties demonstrates that particle-based classification can achieve approximately 67% accuracy, in identifying plume-affected observations but is limited by temporal variability in volcanic plume composition. In contrast, proximity-based approaches more consistently capture plume influence, suggesting that spatial context is critical for effective data flagging. These findings highlight a previously under-recognised source of uncertainty in satellite-derived inland water quality datasets. They support the implementation of proximity-based quality flags to improve data reliability in regions affected by volcanic activity in situations where consistent particle-based observations are unavailable.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Accurate bathymetry in optically shallow waters (OSWs), particularly across coral reef environments, is essential for coastal ecosystem management and benthic habitat monitoring. Satellite-derived bathymetry (SDB) offers a scalable alternative, but reliance on in situ depth measurements constrains its application in data-sparse OSW regions, while the coupling between water-column and benthic signals limits retrieval accuracy. Here, we developed OSW-SDB, a simulation-based SDB model for Case-1 OSWs that is not tailored to a specific region and accounts for variations in benthic reflectance. Using a newly developed benthic reflectance library comprising 2,100 spectra, we generated 200,000 pairs of water depth and remote sensing reflectance (Rrs) by radiative transfer simulations spanning diverse water-column optical properties and benthic reflectance conditions. An XGBoost model was trained based on the simulated dataset using SHAP-selected multispectral features, with 6,711 matched ICESat-2/ATL24 observations additionally used for hyperparameter optimization. OSW-SDB achieved coefficient of determination (R2) values above 0.96 and root mean square error (RMSE) values below 1 m on the simulated training and testing datasets. Two independent simulation-based validations using benthic spectra excluded from model development yielded R2 values of 0.84 and 0.94, and RMSEs of 2.26 and 1.35 m, respectively. Median absolute percentage errors (MdAPEs) were below 15% across all simulated datasets. This model was applied to Sentinel-2/MSI imagery after OSW extraction, producing spatially coherent 10-m bathymetry maps across seven OSW regions. Independent validation using 13,296 ATL24 depths covering 20 reef sites yielded an R2 of 0.80, an RMSE of 0.75 m, and an MdAPE of 26.13%. Additional evaluations using airborne LiDAR and in situ SeaBASS observations, together with comparisons against two existing SDB models, demonstrated stable performance across geographically diverse OSWs. The model is designed for Case-1 OSWs shallower than 20 m, with better performance below 10 m, consistent with the depth range of benthic habitat mapping. OSW-SDB enables practical and transferable bathymetric mapping at 10-m resolution across diverse Case-1 OSWs using only Sentinel-2/MSI imagery, without requiring site-specific bathymetric or benthic priors during application.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Satellite-based wildfire spread studies differ in data organization and evaluation procedures, making model performance difficult to compare. We constructed an event-level benchmark to compare model performance and examine how the models used the available inputs. The benchmark covers California during 2012 to 2021 and combines VIIRS 375 m active-fire observations, MODIS NDVI-derived vegetation information, and ERA5-Land wind fields. Each wildfire event was represented on a fixed 100 × 100 grid. Each sequence sample used ten historical daily steps to predict the next-day satellite-observed fire-state mask. Six spatiotemporal models (CNN-LSTM, Vision Transformer, ConvLSTM, Pairwise-ConvLSTM, Patchwise-ConvLSTM, and ST-GNN) were evaluated using common input definitions, preprocessing procedures, prediction targets, and metrics. Integrated Gradients was applied after training to quantify attributions from the five raster input channels to the next-day model outputs. Pairwise-ConvLSTM achieved the lowest overall RMSE of 0.0495 on the cumulative wildfire dataset. Among the five evaluated Patchwise-ConvLSTM configurations, the 7 × 7 setting achieved the highest observed F1-score (0.955), with precision (0.962) and recall (0.949). On the newly burned-pixel subset, the same configuration achieved precision (0.916), recall (0.888), and F1-score (0.902). The attribution analysis showed larger mean absolute values for vegetation-constrained unburned states (7.23 × 10−5) and prior cumulative fire history (5.13 × 10−5) than for newly detected fire pixels (1.81 × 10−5), the eastward wind component (7.01 × 10−6), and the northward wind component (8.97 × 10−6). The benchmark provides a common framework for assessing predictive performance and model input use in next-day satellite-observed wildfire spread prediction.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Drought increasingly constrains ecosystem functioning in semi-arid regions, yet the nonlinear thresholds through which vegetation restoration regulates carbon–water processes and ecosystem resilience remain poorly understood. In this study, a drought–carbon–water–resilience framework was developed to assess how vegetation restoration affects ecosystem drought resilience in the Mu Us Sandy Land, a representative semi-arid sandy ecosystem in northern China. MODIS products, including MOD17A2H/MOD17A3H for gross and net primary productivity (GPP and NPP), MOD16A2 for evapotranspiration (ET), MOD13A2 for the normalized difference vegetation index (NDVI), MOD11A2 for land surface temperature (LST), and MOD44B for vegetation cover, were used for the period 2001–2020 and harmonized to a 1000-m spatial resolution and an annual temporal scale to quantify water use efficiency (WUE), carbon use efficiency (CUE), the temperature–vegetation drought index (TVDI), ecosystem resistance, recovery, resilience, and a restoration intensity index (RII). The results showed that drought intensity increased slightly over time, while WUE and CUE exhibited contrasting spatial patterns and different sensitivities to drought. At the pixel scale, temporal correlations between annual TVDI and WUE/CUE were positive over 83.74% and 69.78% of the study area, respectively, indicating that carbon–water use efficiency often showed compensatory increases under mild-to-moderate drought conditions. In contrast, the regional drought-gradient analysis revealed threshold-dependent responses of WUE and CUE to increasing TVDI, with critical thresholds at approximately TVDI = 0.67 and TVDI = 0.66, respectively; beyond these thresholds, both water and carbon use efficiency declined rapidly. Strong restoration areas showed higher RSwue and RScue than weak/no restoration and degradation areas, and the proportion of high-resilience areas increased with restoration intensity, particularly for CUE-based resilience. Hierarchical regression further showed that RII provided additional explanatory power for ecosystem resilience after controlling for drought intensity, although its effects differed between water-use and carbon-use processes. These findings indicate that vegetation restoration enhances drought resilience through threshold-dependent carbon–water regulation, but its effectiveness depends on vegetation structure and water availability. Threshold-based monitoring and structure-optimized restoration should therefore be prioritized in semi-arid sandy ecosystem management.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Vegetation dynamics in drylands fundamentally restructure the land-atmosphere interface, yet the resulting surface water-energy coupling across saline-alkali environments remains insufficiently understood due to the complex interplay between climatic aridity and edaphic salinity. By integrating multi-decadal satellite observations with a biogeophysical decomposition framework, we quantified the growing-season impacts of vegetation enhancement (cropland expansion and grassland restoration) alongside desertification across the saline-alkali drylands of northern China from 2003 to 2020. Our results demonstrated that vegetation enhancement induced significant localized surface cooling, peaking in barren-to-cropland conversions (−0.674 ± 0.02 K), followed by grassland-to-cropland conversions (−0.066 ± 0.007 K) and barren-to-grassland restoration (−0.017 ± 0.009 K), whereas desertification exhibited a subtle warming tendency (+0.005 ± 0.0003 K). These thermal responses were primarily governed by non-radiative, evapotranspiration-mediated processes. Furthermore, the salinity-aridity nexus modulated regional energy partitioning through two divergent hydro-edaphic pathways. In reclaimed irrigated cropland, management interventions alleviated ambient matric and osmotic constraints, while intensifying regional water consumption. In contrast, non-irrigated restored grasslands exhibited a functional decoupling between canopy greening and evaporative cooling, reflecting conservative stomatal regulation enforced by compound soil matric and osmotic stresses. The marginal warming accompanying desertification indicated an impaired surface evaporative buffer following vegetation loss and secondary salinization. By elucidating how the salinity-aridity nexus modulates land-surface water-energy coupling, these findings highlight the necessity to align restoration strategies with local hydro-edaphic capacities, balancing the microclimatic cooling of irrigated agriculture against the long-term hydrological sustainability of natural grasslands under accelerating global change.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Proof assistants such as Lean check mathematical proofs down to the axioms, and AI agents have recently made writing such proofs cheap. We describe AutoIP, a Lean library in which classical linear regularization theory, together with the functional analysis it rests on, has been autoformalized by AI agents. A machine-checked proof certifies only the formal statement it proves, so the remaining human task is to confirm that each formal statement says what was intended. We organize the library around this task: a reviewer compares the formal statements with their natural-language counterparts and never needs to read a proof. Our experience is that the risks lie in the statements rather than in the proofs, and that simple automated tests on the statements can expose problems even in the established literature. We illustrate this with a textbook theorem whose hypothesis is more restrictive than asserted. We invite the inverse problems community to extend and build on the library.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Liability adjudication tasks (e.g., legal charge determination) require analyzing event descriptions against domain-specific skills to determine whether a party should bear liability. While Large Language Models (LLMs) have shown promise in this area by leveraging executable skills for interpretable reasoning, existing approaches face three critical limitations: skill libraries become stale as real-world scenarios dynamically evolve, skill descriptions often suffer from a semantic execution gap where LLMs deviate from intended expert logic, and growing skill collections exceed context budgets causing critical skill omission. To address these challenges, we propose Knowledge-Data Dual-Driven Automated Skill Optimization (KDASO), which continuously evolves a skill library through neighborhood-conditioned induction from recent adjudication data, execution-aligned refinement based on LLM trajectory analysis, and context-budgeted routing for scalable skill selection. Experiments across ride-hailing adjudication, legal liability determination, and logical reasoning judgment demonstrate that KDASO significantly outperforms current general-purpose and domain-specific baselines. KDASO further achieves 8.96% improvement when transferring skills to concept-similar scenarios and maintains consistent performance gains through continuous optimization, underscoring the high transferability and stability of generated skills.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reproducible LLM inference requires that a request produces the same output regardless of the other requests in the batch. Existing solutions pay for this in different ways: batch-invariant kernels constrain every operator, and isolating prefill with fixed-shape decode ({our CoRun-style baseline}) gives up shared prefill compute and pads decode. We present BlockLock, which keeps shared batched prefill and pins only the numerical path of the protected request: it fixes the geometry that the request sees, with {two modes: BlockLock-Rows uses fixed-width rows and retains batched decode of all requests; BlockLock-Pack uses terminal-anchored packing with an isolated dummy segment, then extracts the protected request's KV state for target-only decode. Under a stated per-deployment contract, both modes preserve the target's output bitwise when its companions change.} Across five {dense} models (Qwen3-8B/14B, Llama-3.1-8B-Instruct, and Gemma-3-4B/12B-IT) and three single-GPU deployments (NVIDIA A6000, A100, and H100), we evaluate our approach on both tinyBenchmarks and BFCL{; we also include initial tensor-parallel experiments on two NVIDIA A6000 GPUs. Both modes preserve target sequences and Top20-logit bits in the reported tests.} At equal delivered work, {BlockLock-Rows} reduces latency by 53.5--55.0\% and improves throughput by 115.2--122.2\% relative to vLLM Batch-Invariant; compared with batch-invariant kernels, it needs no custom kernel implementations and constrains only the protected requests.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Reinforcement learning post-training improves the capabilities of large language models, but generating training responses is a major computational bottleneck. In mixture-of-experts models, workers that finish their assigned responses may remain occupied because their experts are still needed by workers processing longer responses. Consequently, approximately one-third of occupied worker time in the baseline occurs after local response generation finishes. Here we present FlexFloor, a system that enables earlier resource release during synchronous response generation while preserving the expert computation required by ongoing responses. FlexFloor jointly adapts response scheduling and the active group of workers. It uses historical response lengths and memory constraints to guide batching and prompt placement, then progressively releases workers while maintaining expert availability and correct communication. Compared with the verl baseline, FlexFloor generates approximately 65.40% more response tokens per unit of occupied computing time and increases response generation throughput by 52.74%, with little observed variation in rollout reward. By making reinforcement learning post-training more efficient, FlexFloor supports faster development and iterative improvement of large language models and AI agents.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Enterprises run business transactions through multi-agent AI systems spanning clouds, large language models (LLMs), orchestration harnesses, and systems of record. Finance receives AI cost only at settlement grain, aggregated by SKU and charge period. The business question (what did this order, claim, or case cost in AI?) cannot be answered from billing data, because providers never see enterprise transaction boundaries. The FinOps Open Cost and Usage Specification (FOCUS) gives billing data one schema across providers but remains settlement-grain even as it adds AI token and requester attribution. We formalize this grain mismatch, derive seven requirements, and propose the Two-Ledger Model: a settlement ledger of FOCUS-conformant billing data joined to an attribution ledger of invocation-grain usage records built on OpenTelemetry GenAI telemetry. A layered correlation spine (trace context, A2A/MCP metadata, business-key correlation, probabilistic matching) assigns each record to its business transaction with recorded method and confidence; a versioned rate card prices usage in near real time; period reconciliation ties both ledgers to the invoice, and the unexplained residual identifies shadow AI. Costs are reported per order, claim, or outcome with agentic cost metrics. A simulation with known ground truth shows the spine sustaining cost coverage above 99% as trace propagation falls to zero, the reconciliation residual recovering planted unmetered spend within 0.24 percentage points while flagging provider billing errors, and mid-flight gating cutting p99 transaction cost by up to 61% at a 2-4% false-pause rate. We propose the attribution ledger as an invocation-grain FOCUS profile for standardization.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Artificial intelligence (AI) systems have ushered in a new age of proof abundance, but helping humans understand these proofs requires new attention to the differences in how AI agents and humans pursue a proof-making task. We present "simulated collaboration", a methodology that allows us to examine two key questions: (1) the relative attention AI agents pay to surface syntax or, conversely, deeper underlying semantics, and (2) how and where AI agent preferences for what to do next diverge from human preferences. On a set of nine major human collaborations in Lean with 4.7M definitions across more than 6K commits, we find that, over the course of proof construction, AI agents (1) are far more strongly drawn than humans to explicitly marked gaps; (2) show a relative aversion to densely connected proof centers and to complex or surprising proof goals; and (3) are relatively attracted to surprising proof bodies, which they tend to regularize toward baseline. Our results highlight important blind spots and potential interventions for future human-AI collaboration in mathematics.

</details>

Abstract (英文): The policy that converts runtime estimates of an autonomous agent's condition into operational authority is rarely specified or measured. We empirically characterize the Trust Responsive Authorization Policy (TRAP), which maps an Authorization Readiness Score (ARS), a weighted combination of runtime trust, operational stability, and contextual risk, onto discrete authorization levels through an asymmetric rule: escalation requires consecutive observations above a band edge plus a margin, while a sharp drop in readiness restricts authority at once. On one matched set of 100 Monte Carlo traces, we compare TRAP with static, trust-only, risk-only, and symmetric policies. Under abrupt degradation, TRAP restricts at onset and re-escalates sooner after recovery than the symmetric policy, with the highest and, descriptively, least variable stability among the adaptive policies, although more of its post-recovery escalations occur below the selected trust threshold. Paired bootstrap intervals exclude zero for the remaining differences. In one-factor sweeps, recovery time rises with the escalation margin and run length, shifts with the score weights, and is unchanged by the restriction threshold. In an exploratory post-freeze experiment, when the same degradation is spread over 5-20 intervals, TRAP restricts only on the 4 traces that retain a single-interval drop above that threshold; averaged over all 100 traces, it holds authority above what readiness supports for 15.9-18.9 of the 20 degradation intervals, against 0.8 under abrupt degradation and about 10 for the symmetric policy. Its restriction advantage therefore depends on degradation being abrupt. All reported values are traceable to the publicly available, versioned research release.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Evolution has shaped intelligence for billions of years. Artificial intelligence (AI) now raises a striking possibility: intelligence may begin to direct its own evolution. As AI agents become capable of modifying and evaluating their own behavior, how quickly could they improve, and how can that improvement remain safe? We develop a theoretical framework comparing Darwinian, Lamarckian, and agent-directed evolution following environmental change. Darwinian evolution relies on natural selection, Lamarckian evolution adds inherited learning, and agent-directed evolution allows agents to generate and evaluate behavioral variants and choose how extensively to search. When learning is sufficiently effective, these mechanisms exhibit progressively faster adaptation. Generating more variants accelerates adaptation, while optimal search effort depends on learning ability, problem difficulty, and experimentation costs. However, an agent's ability to direct its improvement does not ensure that improvements are evaluated against the intended objective. When the evaluation target is misspecified, additional search accelerates convergence toward the wrong behavior, even as measured performance improves. In recursive self-improvement, such errors may persist across generations, posing challenges for AI safety. Independent evaluation and oversight must keep pace with autonomous AI development. The central challenge is not simply to accelerate the evolution of intelligence, but to preserve humanity's ability to guide its direction, limit its speed, and intervene when necessary.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Once an agent is live, teams want to know whether it is doing its job, not just whether it throws errors. Yet most evaluation is one-off: a benchmark run, a look at traces, a single score. Its conclusions neither check themselves nor reach the agent. We propose closed-loop evaluation, an evaluation method for deployed agents. It models evaluation as performance estimation under biased raters and censored outcomes, and it requires every level's conclusion to trace back to atomic data and to be computed by versioned rules. The method covers four levels of analysis (span, trace, conversation and engagement), measures along a spine of Execution, Substance, Trajectory and Results, treats Conduct as a constraint throughout, and uses Provenance and Adjudication components to certify the evidence and the evaluators. Evaluator agents generated by an Agent Factory carry these components, each with a domain knowledge pack and an adapter harness. Two loops close the evaluation: an inner loop audits the evidence, rules and metrics themselves, and an outer loop uses per-component regression checks to drive agent improvement. We also give a pipeline that runs this process automatically. Using the business-analysis agent SharpInsight as a case and porting the method to public conversation data, we show how problems differ across levels and how evidence-and-rule judgment is more reliable than holistic scoring (ICC 0.86 vs. 0.81; kappa 0.81 vs. 0.63).

</details>

Abstract (英文): Discovering cooperative behavior in multi-agent reinforcement learning (MARL) is challenging due to the combinatorial complexity of joint state-action spaces, which hinders the emergence of coordinated behaviors from trial-and-error alone. Intrinsic rewards are often used to aid discovery, but naively combining them with team objectives can distort the learning signal, compromising task performance. In this paper, we propose EFXPLORER, a constrained exploration framework that maximizes exploration objectives subject to a constraint that preserves established task performance. We solve the resulting problem using an epigraph reformulation that introduces adaptive exploration budgets. This approach separates intrinsic rewards from task objectives and regulates exploration through task feasibility. To further encourage diverse and temporally extended exploration, we incorporate a successor distance-based intrinsic reward that captures long-horizon dependencies. Empirically, our method outperforms strong baselines and induces novel cooperative strategies across SMAX, VMAS, and MPE benchmark suites.

</details>

Abstract (英文): A verified Dafny postcondition certifies that a formula holds, but it does not guarantee that the result was reached through genuine independent reasoning. We introduce a cross-agent formalization protocol where a Specifier model translates clinical trial criteria into a Dafny predicate, and an Implementer model writes an operational method checked against it. A same-model control establishes that even with no capability gap, independent re-extraction from identical text agrees only 59.3% of the time. Evaluating four model configurations on 96 clinical trial documents, we find that agreement is highly sensitive to how model capability is allocated across the two roles: the GPT-4.1-nano/GPT-4.1-mini configuration achieves 53.0% agreement when the stronger model is the Implementer, but only 14.3% when the roles are reversed. The strongest tested configuration, GPT-5.6-luna/GPT-5.4-mini, improves agreement to 34.4% but remains below the same-model baseline and is dominated by direct contradiction (42.7%). We also detect a critical verification artifact in which a weaker Implementer satisfies the postcondition by invoking the Specifier's predicate directly; after structural auditing, corrected agreement falls from 14.3% to 13.2%. Our results show that verification success alone is insufficient evidence of independent cross-agent agreement, and that model capability does not guarantee agreement when formalizing the same source independently.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Symbolic models offer a powerful account of cognition, but their scope has historically been limited by the need for hand-engineering and computational tractability. We show how advances in artificial intelligence (AI) can scale symbolic modeling and help overcome these engineering limitations. We introduce Behavior-Conditioned Symbolic Synthesis (BCSS)---an approach for automated search of interpretable, deterministic symbolic programs that verifiably reproduce behavior---and apply it to model both human and machine behavior across hundreds of challenging reasoning problems. Analyzing these programs reveals that both people and machines tend to oversimplify, adopt rules that are partial explanations for their observations, and misinfer dependencies between problem features. These shared signatures are modulated by available resources, with reasoning effort predicting the extent to which machines simplify and explain their observations. Together, these results point to underlying mechanisms reminiscent of satisficing in both people and machines, and illustrate how BCSS offers a general approach for interpreting and comparing rule-based reasoning of humans and black-box AI systems.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Large language models (LLMs) are increasingly used as automated judges for evaluating the behavior of chatbots and other AI systems. In deployment, however, their agreement with the intended human evaluation policy may degrade as user interactions and score distributions change. Existing automatic prompt optimization methods are primarily studied as one-shot procedures on static development sets and therefore provide limited guidance for maintaining LLM judges over time. We frame this problem as judge drift and introduce PromptOps, a closedloop framework for monitoring and iteratively recalibrating LLM-as-a-Judge (LLMaaJ) systems. PromptOps separates four operational decisions: when to optimize, which optimization procedure to apply, which feedback records to use, and how to update the judge prompt. We instantiate the framework with five prompt optimization methods: GEPA, SIMBA, Rubric Synthesis, and exemplar selection using either simulated annealing or a Tree-structured Parzen Estimator. Our experiments demonstrate that iterative prompt optimization can recalibrate LLM judges and recover performance after controlled shifts in the referencescore distribution. However, effective maintenance is inherently contextdependent: neither optimization algorithm choice nor update timing generalizes universally across settings. These findings establish prompt optimization for deployed LLM judges as an inherently iterative maintenance problem and demonstrate that PromptOps provides a practical abstraction for studying and implementing this process.

</details>

Abstract (英文): Power matters for AI safety. Optimal agents tend to acquire power across a wide range of objectives (instrumental convergence). As we embed AI agents into socio-technical systems, humans risk gradual disempowerment. Both concerns call for a formal, quantitative measure of power. Two such measures in the context of Markov games are information-theoretic empowerment, the capacity of the channel from an agent's actions to future states, and capability-based individual power, a measure of goal attainment built from the probabilities that a human would attain each goal in a family of candidates were they pursuing it. We show where the two meet. From individual power, Fano's inequality yields a floor $B(s)$. This floor is a lower bound on what we term goal revelation, the mutual information $\mathbb{I}(G;ξ\mid s)$ between the human's latent goal and the realised trajectory. Empowerment caps revelation from above, so that $B(s) \le \mathbb{I}(G;ξ\mid s) \le \mathbb{I}(\mathbf{A}_h \to ξ\mid s) \le \mathfrak{E}_h(s)$. Each quantity can be large while the one below it stays at zero, highlighting the following failure modes. An assistant maximising empowerment can prefer an interface offering broad influence over goal-irrelevant degrees of freedom to one that lets the human attain every goal they might hold. An assistant maximising revelation cannot tell a working interface from one rewired so that every goal yields the wrong outcome, since relabelling goals leaves mutual information unchanged while zeroing every attainment probability. Positive guarantees therefore come only from the attainment end of the chain. A satisfaction floor, computable from two statistics of interaction logs, offers a way to monitor the power users retain in a deployed system, and simulations in random environments show the three quantities are correlated but far from interchangeable.

</details>

Abstract (英文): When a tropical cyclone (TC) threatens several ports, preparedness planning depends on anticipating which ports are likely to be affected and where disruptions may last longest. Limited historical observations at individual ports and uncertainty in landfall location make these relative impacts difficult to estimate. We use a two-stage recommendation framework that first selects candidate ports based on their proximity to the expected landfall location and then ranks them by their estimated number of impact days. Using AIS-derived impact days for 37 ports and 31 TCs in the Gulf of Mexico study region, we compare three ways of representing port--cyclone interactions: pairwise feature interactions, tree-based feature combinations, and explicit feature crosses combined with learned embeddings. In tests on Hurricanes Nicole and Ian, the retrieval stage consistently identifies all observed affected ports under landfall uncertainty. Among the ranking models, Wide & Deep Learning achieves the closest agreement with the observed port rankings. Its mean ranking agreement exceeds that of the distance-based baseline by 85.5% for Nicole and 55.5% for Ian and is less sensitive to uncertainty in the landfall location. These rankings could help port authorities prioritize post-cyclone inspections and allocate recovery resources across ports threatened by the same event.

</details>
